## Abstract
Cardiomyopathy represents one of the primary causes of mortality in Duchenne muscular dystrophy (DMD) patients. Specific cardiac therapeutic guidelines are lacking, mostly in advanced stages of DMD. We report the results of a retrospective analysis of clinical data: age, ejection fraction (EF), cardiac therapy and detailed functional assessments of 82 DMD patients (age range:3-36 years, mean:15,8) followed at the Centre of Messina and seen between March 2018 and March 2023.
We divided the cohort in three subgroups according to age: 15 patients (18%) were younger than 10 years (age range:3-9, mean:6,5), only one of them was taking ACEi.
Forty-two patients (51%) were between 10 and 20 years (mean:14,5), 36/42 were in different cardiac therapies (ACEi, beta-blockers or ivabradine). Twenty-five patients were older than 20 years (30%, age range:20-36 years, mean:23,5), of them 21 were in polytherapy, furthermore one patient had a cardioverter-defibrillator implanted and two underwent surgical implantation of a ventricular assist device.
Mean EF (SD) in each group was respectively 68%(4%), 60%(6,8%) and 46%(11%)Moreover, we describe the 5 years follow-up of 24 DMD paediatric patients (at first evaluation age range:3-18 years; mean 9,6). They showed impairment of longitudinal strain (median value -20.6, IQR 3), evaluated by speckle tracking echocardiography, while we did not observe an impairment in EF at baseline, neither a significant reduction over time.
Here, we describe the severity and progression of myocardial involvement in a large cohort of DMD patients. We confirm that EF is not sufficient to detect the entity of cardiac involvement nor the possible impact of therapies.

## Abstract
Duchenne muscular dystrophy (DMD) is an X-linked neuromuscular disease due to pathogenic variants in the DMD gene causing the absence of dystrophin protein. Dystrophin function is not only limited to muscle but is also relevant in several brain circuits. Pathogenic variations disrupting both full-length (Dp427) and short DMD isoforms as Dp140 and Dp71 are known to impact on the neurobehavioral phenotypes.
We assessed the topography of DMD isoforms by Basescope analysis in FFPE tissues from human male adult cerebellum, vermis, paracentral gyrus, corpus callosum, cingulate gyrus, and hippocampus, obtained from Edinburgh Brain-Tissue Bank.
The more abundant isoforms in studied brain areas were Dp427c, Dp427m, Dp427p2, Dp140, Dp71 and Dp40, based on a preliminary Real-time PCR screeningBaseScope showed that these isoforms were transcribed in all brain areas with a different abundance among donors. Three main DMD isoforms clusters were identified, including i) low represented, as Dp427m and Dp427p2, ii) medium represented, as Dp140 and Dp40, which are mainly localized in hippocampus and cerebellum, and iii) highly represented, as Dp427c and Dp71, which are enriched in hippocampus, cerebellum, and paracentral gyrus. Vermis, corpus callosum and cingulate gyrus were the areas with lowest isoforms’ representation. We could also recognize isoforms with cell- layer-specific localization in cerebellum, vermis, hippocampus and paracentral gyrusWe defined spatial and topographic representation of DMD isoforms in human adult brain. The enrichment in Dp140 isoform in cerebellum and in Dp427c and Dp71 in hippocampus are well aligned with the brain phenotypes observed in individuals affected by DMD.

## Abstract
Dista-Myo is an international multicenter longitudinal study funded by AFM Telethon, evaluating patients with a molecularly defined diagnosis of distal (DM) and myofibrillar myopathy (MFM). It was designed to characterize the profile of motor function impairment in these disorders combining functional scales with new digital biomarkers and to assess disease progression over 12-months.
Patients were assessed by Motor Function Measure (MFM32), 6-Minute Walk Test (6MWT), Timed Up and Go (TUG) and Nine holes-peg test (9HPT), as functional outcome measures; technological outcome measures included Myotools, for muscle strength evaluation, and gait analysis while wearing Loadsol® insoles.
Thirty-seven patients (mean age 47.62±15.21 years) were studied at baseline. Ninety-two% showed reduced values on MFM32 total score, with 35% showing this reduction in the distal (D3) domain. Fifty-three% underperformed on 6MWT. Most patients showed distal muscle strength impairment when tested with MyoTools (66% at ankle dorsiflexion, 83% at ankle plantar flexion and 60% at hand grip level), while manual dexterity was impaired in 20% of patients. Indexes derived from gait analysis (“foot strike”, “foot off” and stance phase duration) significantly differed between patients and controls and correlated with ankle muscle strength and functional scoresThe cohort of patients with distal weakness covers a large phenotypic and severity spectrum, ranging from normal-performing to non-ambulant patients. However, our baseline results suggest that a significant decrease in global motor function is present in the majority of DM and MFM patients and that the impairment in distal muscles and gait ability can be efficiently captured by digital outcome measures.

## Abstract
Our collaborative study was aimed at providing a proof of principle of the efficacy of CRISPR/Cas-based correction of LMNA or EMD gene mutations in myoblasts from patients affected by Emery-Dreifuss Muscular Dystrophy (EDMD2 or EDMD1). The approach we used to correct the mutated EMD gene was cytosine base editing. While EDMD1 fibroblast and myoblast nuclei examined in this study were emerin negative due to premature stop codons in the mutated sequence, around 60% of gene edited EDMD1 patient myoblasts showed emerin localization in the nuclear membrane, indicating proper transcription and translation of the corrected gene. Further, emerin expression and nuclear membrane localization were restored in up to 90 % of EDMD1 fibroblasts subjected to base editing. CRISPR/Cas correction of mutated LMNA gene was also obtained in EDMD2 fibroblasts. In corrected EDMD1 or EDMD2 cell cultures, the percentage of honeycomb structures, an established signature of laminopathic nuclei, was significantly reduced. Moreover, loss of perinuclear pericentrin, which had been observed in EDMD1 and EDMD2 myotubes, was rescued by CRISPR/Cas correction of mutated EMD gene. These results indicate functional improvement of EDMD1 muscle cells by CRISPR/Cas-based gene editing. We suggest exploring proper delivery tools for gene editing machineries, in view of a more translational approach to gene editing for Emery-Dreifuss Muscular Dystrophy.

Acta MyologicaActa MyolHuman mutated MYOT and CRYAB genes cause a myopathic phenotype in zebrafish
MarchettoG CannoneE GuglielmiV TobiaC GnuttiB CisternaB BarbonA ToninP SchiavoneM VattemiG 1 Department of Neurosciences, Biomedicine and Movement Sciences, Division of Neuropathology, University of Verona, Verona, Italy2 Department of Molecular and Translational Medicine, Division of Biology and Genetics, University of Brescia, Brescia, Italy3 Department of Neurosciences, Biomedicine and Movement Sciences, Section of Anatomy and Histology, University of Verona, Verona, Italy162023421 Suppl 14141–113©2023 Gaetano Conte Academy - Mediterranean Society of Myology, Naples, ItalyThis is an open access article distributed in accordance with the CC-BY-NC-ND (Creative Commons Attribution-NonCommercial-NoDerivatives 4.0 International) license. ## Abstract
Myofibrillar myopathies (MFM) are a group of hereditary neuromuscular disorders sharing common histological features of myofibrillar organization impairment, Z-disk disintegration, and accumulation of degradation products into protein aggregates. MFM are caused by mutations in several genes that encode either for structural proteins such as MYOT, LBD3/ZASP, FLNC and DES, or for molecular chaperones such as CRYAB and BAG3. However, the mechanisms by which mutated genes result in myofibrillar degeneration and protein aggregation are not understood and there are no available therapies for MFM. To unveil the role of myotilin and αB-crystallin in the pathogenesis of MFM, we injected zebrafish fertilized eggs at one-cell stage with expression plasmids harboring human cDNA sequences of wildtype or p.Ser95Ile mutated MYOT and wildtype or p.Gly154Ser mutated CRYAB. We evaluated the effects on fish survival, motor behavior, muscle structure and development. We found that transgenic zebrafish overexpressing either wildtype or mutated genes, showed morphological defects which were more severe for mutants which developed a myopathic phenotype consistent with that of human myofibrillar myopathy including the formation of protein aggregates. These results suggest that pathogenic mutations in myotilin and αB-crystallin genes associated with MFM can cause structural and functional impairment of the skeletal muscle in zebrafish, thereby making this non-mammalian organism a powerful model to dissect MFM disease pathogenesis and to find possible druggable targets

## Abstract
Myopathic-Ehlers-Danlos-Syndrome (mEDS), a combination of connective and muscle tissue abnormalities, was recently associated with COL12A1 gene variants. To date only 17 families have been described, here we enlarge this cohort of patients and focus on a characteristic sign associated with this novel phenotype.
We observed three independent families characterized by soft skin, scoliosis, joint hyperlaxity/hypermobility with recurring dislocations, axial/proximal/distal muscle hypotrophy. All these families were characterized by rectus femoris atrophy. Searching for causative mutations, we performed Whole Exome Sequencing (WES), which detected two splicing variants, c.8415+1G > A and c.8319+1G > T (NM_004370.6) in COL12A1 gene, in two of three analysed families. RNA analysis confirmed the involvement of these variants in the skipping of exon 56 and 55, respectively. Protein effect was in both cases a COL2 protein domain deletion. Moreover, fibroblast analysis showed a dysregulation of COL12A1 cellular localization. Furthermore the third family, carried the known c.3309G > A, p.Pro1103 = (NM_000393.5) in the COL5A2 gene. RNA analysison patient fibroblasts, confirmed its involvement in aberrant splicing, leading to exon 46 skipping. Interestingly, String analysis showed strong association between COL12A1 and COL5A2. Ongoing study on patient fibroblasts could allow us to reveal a dysregulation in COL12A1, as consequence of COL5A2 mutation, thus establishing a functional connection between COL12A1 and COL5A2 protein. If this hypothesis will be confirmed we could speculate about a connection between patient phenotype and COL12A1 aberrant expression. Overall, these data improve the characterization of the pathogenic phenotype associates with COL12A1 dysfunction, defining a novel syndrome, the Ehlers-Danlos/Rectus Femoris myopathy.

CheliM BortolaniS RolleE VicinoA BonannoS MonginiTE TascaG MaggiL 1 Neuroimmunology and Neuromuscular Diseases Unit, Foundation IRCCS Neurological Institute Carlo Besta, Milano, Italy2 Department of Neurosciences Rita Levi Montalcini, University of Torino, Torino, Italy3 Unità Operativa Complessa di Neurologia, Fondazione Policlinico Universitario A. ## Abstract
Myofibrillar myopathy (MFM) identifies a group of hereditary myopathies characterized by disorganized myofibrils with cytoplasmic inclusions. Distal myopathies are a genetically heterogeneous group of muscle disorders with prevalent weakness in the distal part of upper and lower limbs. Both diseases showed phenotypic heterogeneity and variable natural history. Aim of this study is to longitudinally assess the disease progression of MFM and DM in an Italian cohort of patients.
Patients diagnosed with MFM and DM were prospectively enrolled in three Italian neuromuscular centres, and they were evaluated on a yearly basis. The evaluation included full neurological examination with North Star Ambulatory Assessment and timed tests (get up from floor and chair) and IBM-FRS.
We present here the data for the first year (T1) of follow-up of the included cohort. 58 patients (32 females) were enrolled. Median age at first evaluation was 49 years (range 18-84). Mutated genes were DES and GNE (n = 8), DNAJB6 (n = 7), DYSF and PLIN4 (n = 6). Mean IBM-FRS and NSAA baseline scores were 30.5±9.2 and 20±12.1, respectivelyThirty-one patients were revaluated at 12 months. At this timepoint, mean ΔIBM-FRS was -0.35±3.1: 7 patients improved (ΔIBM-FRS: 3.5±2.6), 9 patients remained stable, and 15 patients worsened (ΔIBM-FRS: -2.5±2). ΔNSAA score was -1.5±2.1: 14 patients remained stable, 15 worsened (ΔNSAA: -3±2) and 2 improved (1 point each).
Our preliminary data suggest a disease stability or mild worsening according to NSAA and, to a lesser extent, IBM-FRS at one-year follow-up.

## Abstract
Asymptomatic or mildly symptomatic persistent hyperckemia albeit extremely common represents a diagnostic challenge for being a subtle and unspecific manifestation of different muscular dystrophies, metabolic conditions and several other neuromuscular disorders. The DAG1 gene encodes the dystroglycan, a dystrophin-associated glycoprotein (DAG), known to be responsible for a form of recessive limb girdle muscular dystrophy (LGMDR16) and a muscle-eye-brain disease with or without leukodystrophy. Recently a novel 1-bp deletion variant in DAG1 has been associate to asymptomatic hyper-CK-emia in a single Japanese family.
We were able to reach a definite genetic diagnosis in patients with dominant clinically-associated mutation in DAG1. Ampliseq/Ion Torrent PGM technology identified 5 variants of DAG1 in a total of 7 cases all presenting with elevated CK (value ranged from da 4 a 10 V) without overt muscle weakness nor brain abnormalities Validation and segregation of selected rare variants was obtained by Sanger. All variants determine either premature stop or frameshift effect on mRNA and pathogenetic effects of variants have been attributed according to ACMG guidelines. We confirmed the absence of the SNV in 5000 in house exomes Gaslini database (BioGeAR), with MAV < = 0.01. In muscle biopsy of 2 cases we detected a reduced expression of alpha dystroglycanThese findings confirm previous report and suggest that heterozygous mutations in DAG1 gene can be responsible for asymptomatic/pauci-symptomatic hyperckemia in children and phenotype generally milder than the recessive form in adult, with important implication for clinical diagnosis and genetic counseling.

## Abstract
Establish efficacy of rozanolixizumab, a humanised IgG4 monoclonal antibody that inhibits FcRn, in patients with generalised myasthenia gravis (gMG) across subgroupsThe double-blind, placebo-controlled, Phase 3 MycarinG study (MG0003/NCT03971422) randomised patients (≥18 years, MGFA Class II–IVa AChR/MuSK autoantibody-positive gMG) 1:1:1 to weekly subcutaneous rozanolixizumab 7mg/kg, rozanolixizumab 10mg/kg or placebo for 6 weeks. The primary endpoint was least-squares mean (LSM) change from baseline (CFB) to Day 43 in MG-ADL score. Post-hoc subgroup analyses included number of prior MG therapies (excluding acetylcholinesterase inhibitors) and baseline disease severity and disease duration. Treatment-emergent adverse events (TEAEs) were assessed in the overall populationPatients (N = 200) received rozanolixizumab 7mg/kg (n = 66), 10mg/kg (n = 67) or placebo (n = 67). In the overall population, LSM CFB at Day 43 was –3.4, –3.4 and –0.8 for rozanolixizumab 7mg/kg, 10mg/kg and placebo, respectively. Mean observed CFB in MG-ADL at Day 43 was greater for rozanolixizumab groups than placebo in subgroups of: ≥1 prior therapy (n = 163): -3.2, -3.3 and -1.0 for rozanolixizumab 7mg/kg, 10mg/kg and placebo, respectively; ≥2 prior therapies (n = 84): -2.5, -3.0 and -0.8; baseline QMG ≤15 (n = 110): -3.7, -2.6 and -0.6; baseline QMG > 15 (n = 90): -3.0, -4.0 and -0.7; baseline disease duration < 4 years (n = 78): -2.9, -3.1 and -0.6; baseline disease duration ≥4 years (n = 122): -3.8, -3.3 and -0.7. TEAEs occurred in 52 (81.3%), 57 (82.6%) and 45 (67.2%) patients, respectively; most were mild or moderateRozanolixizumab treatment demonstrated greater reductions in MG-ADL score than placebo in a broad range of patients with gMGFunding: UCB Pharma.

## Abstract
Long-term data from RAISE-XT (NCT04225871), a Phase 3, multicentre, open-label extension study, will enhance understanding of the safety and efficacy of the C5 inhibitor zilucoplan in patients with generalised myasthenia gravis (gMG).
Adults (≥18– < 75 years) with acetylcholine receptor autoantibody-positive gMG who completed a qualifying zilucoplan study (NCT03315130/NCT04115293) selfadministered daily subcutaneous zilucoplan 0.3mg/kg. Primary outcome: incidence of treatmentemergent adverse events (TEAEs). Secondary outcomes included change from qualifying study baseline to Extension Week 12 (Week E12) in MG Activities of Daily Living (MGADL) score, MG-ADL and Quantitative MG (QMG) responder rates (≥3-point and ≥5-point reduction without rescue therapy, respectively) and minimal symptom expression (MSE; MG-ADL 0/1)104/199 RAISE-XT patients continued zilucoplan from their parent study (zilucoplan group); 95 switched to zilucoplan from placebo (placebo-switch group). Median exposure at data cut-off: 253 days (range 29–1434). TEAEs occurred in 169 (84.9%) patients; 46 (23.1%) patients experienced a serious TEAE. Week E12 least squares mean changes in MG-ADL score for zilucoplan and placebo-switch groups (after 24 and 12 weeks of zilucoplan, respectively): -6.30 (95% CI: -7.44, -5.15); -6.32 (95% CI: -8.00, -4.65). MG-ADL responder rates improved from RAISE-XT baseline through Week E12 (zilucoplan: 75.0% to 80.3% of patients; placebo-switch: 52.2% to 79.7%); QMG responder rates were similarly improved. MSE responder rate increased through Week E12 (zilucoplan: 19.6% to 26.8% of patients; placebo-switch: 7.8% to 29.0%)Zilucoplan demonstrated a favourable long-term safety profile and sustained efficacy. Responder rates improved from RAISE-XT baseline in zilucoplan and placebo-switch groups. Funding: UCB Pharma

## Abstract
Efgartigimod PH20 subcutaneous (SC) showed noninferiority for total IgG reduction compared to efgartigimod IV (ADAPT-SC), demonstrating similar clinical improvements in patients with generalized myasthenia gravis. Patients completing ADAPT-SC/ADAPT+ were eligible for the ongoing open-label extension study, ADAPT-SC+, which is evaluating long-term safety/tolerability and efficacy of efgartigimod PH20 SCEfgartigimod PH20 SC 1000 mg (coformulated with recombinant human hyaluronidase PH20) was administered in cycles of 4 weekly injections. Subsequent cycles were initiated ≥28 days from last dose based on clinical evaluation. Efficacy was assessed utilizing the Myasthenia Gravis Activities of Daily Living (MG-ADL) scale.
As of March 2022, 164 patients received ≥1 dose of efgartigimod PH20 SC, and ~3 cycles over a mean (SD) study duration of 170(59) days. Adverse events were predominantly mild/moderate with injection site erythema (25.6%), headache (15.2%), and COVID-19 (11.6%) most frequently observed. All injection site reactions (ISRs) were mild/moderate, with none leading to treatment discontinuation. ISRs typically occurred within 24 hours of administration, resolved spontaneously, and incidence decreased with subsequent cycles. Two deaths were reported (metastatic renal cancer, COVID-19), neither were considered efgartigimod-related. Improvement from cycle baseline in MG-ADL total score (mean[SE] improvement at week 4: –4.0[0.25]) was observed in cycle 1 with consistent, repeatable improvements seen in subsequent cycles. Onset speed, durability, and repeatability of MG-ADL improvements were similar to efgartigimod IV treatment during ADAPT/ADAPT+.
Multiple cycles of efgartigimod PH20 SC treatment were well tolerated with no new safety signals identified, demonstrating safety and efficacy profiles consistent with ADAPT/ADAPT+.

Acta MyologicaActa MyolIn vitro effects of Rituximab on peripheral B cell subsets of patients with Myasthenia Gravis
MorroniJ SabatelliE MariniS FalsoS IorioR 1Fondazione Policlinico Universitario A. Gemelli IRCCS. UOC Neurologia, Rome, Italy162023421 Suppl 14141–113©2023 Gaetano Conte Academy - Mediterranean Society of Myology, Naples, ItalyThis is an open access article distributed in accordance with the CC-BY-NC-ND (Creative Commons Attribution-NonCommercial-NoDerivatives 4.0 International) license. ## Abstract
Myasthenia gravis (MG) is a B cell-mediated autoimmune neuromuscular disorder caused by autoantibodies targeting proteins expressed at the motor end-plate. In 80% of MG patients autoantibodies binding the Acetylcholine Receptor (AChR-Ab) are detected. Rituximab (RTX), a chimeric monoclonal antibody specific to the CD20 B-cell surface antigen, is a potentially effective treatment for MG patients. However, RTX appears more beneficial for patients with recent-onset MG than those with longer disease duration.
We examined the in vitro effects of RTX on B-cell subsets isolated from the peripheral blood of healthy controls (HC, n = 4) and MG patients with AChR-Ab and varying disease duration: short (< 2 years from disease onset, MG-SD, n = 4) and long (> 5 years from disease onset, MG-LD, n = 4). Peripheral blood mononuclear cells were incubated with different doses of RTX, followed by rabbit complement to induce RTX-mediated killing of the B cells.
We first set RTX dosage for subsequent analysis at 10ug/ml, as higher concentrations often led to B cell depletion. Even though RTX exerted a similar effect on B cells isolated from HC and MG-SD, with naïve B cells representing the most susceptible population, we could observe that B cells isolated from MG-LD patients were more resistant to RTX-mediated killing. Interestingly, the population that showed the strongest resistance to RTX in MG-LD patients was DN, followed by antibody-secreting cells (ASC). It has been shown that DN can differentiate into ASC precursors, suggesting that this population may play an important role in MG pathogenesis and poor response to RTX, warranting further investigation.

PuglieseA BarbacciaA BiasiniF MusumeciO MessinaS ToscanoA RodolicoC 1 Clinical and Experimental Medicine Department, University of Messina, Messina, Italy2
European Reference Network-NeuroMuscular Disease, Messina, Italy162023421 Suppl 14141–113©2023 Gaetano Conte Academy - Mediterranean Society of Myology, Naples, ItalyThis is an open access article distributed in accordance with the CC-BY-NC-ND (Creative Commons Attribution-NonCommercial-NoDerivatives 4.0 International) license. ## Abstract
Lambert-Eaton myasthenic syndrome (LEMS) is a rare neuromuscular junction disorder, mediated by paraneoplastic (P-LEMS) or primary auto-immune mechanisms (NT-LEMS). Patients typically present proximal muscle weakness, autonomic dysfunctions, and reduced tendon reflexes. High frequency repetitive nerve stimulation (RNS) shows an incremental response of compound muscle action potential (CMAP) amplitude. Furthermore, antibodies against presynaptic P/Q-type voltage-gated calcium channels (VGCC-Ab) are detected in 85–90% of patientsWe report herein a cohort of 12 patients affected by LEMS, to describe clinical, neurophysiological, and therapeutic features along a median follow-up of 7,5 years (range 1-25 years).
Our cohort includes 8 males and 4 females, with a mean age of 51,3 years old (SD ±17,5; range 10-80 years) at onset, characterized by difficulty in raising from a chair and in climbing stairs (12/12 patients), and dysautonomia (10/12 patients). Small cell lung carcinoma was diagnosed simultaneously with LEMS in one patient, who died for cerebral metastasis 2 years later. Interestingly, another patient developed xerostomia, bulbar symptoms, and lower legs muscle weakness three months after SARS-CoV-2 infection. All patients presented an increase of CMAP amplitude from a distal muscle after maximal muscle contraction. Six patients were tested positive for VGCC-Abs. All patients have been treated with 3,4-diaminopyridine, in addition with prednisone in eight cases and azathioprine in two of them. Moreover, two patients needed rescue therapy such as plasmapheresis and immunoglobulin at the onset or during the disease. After therapy, seven patients presented an improvement of at least 2 points in MG-ADLLEMS is a rare and insidious disorder, frequently misdiagnosed due to the non-specific clinical features. Neurophysiological studies and VGCC-Abs can allow a correct diagnosis to early detect a possible associated neoplasm and to start therapy, impacting positively on patients’ quality of life and survival.

## Abstract
Rituximab (RTX) is a chimeric monoclonal anti-CD20 antibody that had shown good effectiveness and safety in generalized Myasthenia Gravis (gMG) patients, and is currently recommended as a therapeutic option in MuSK-MG in recent consensus guidelines update. Yet, evidence indicating its most appropriate use is still scarce.
We retrospectively evaluated a cohort of gMG patients treated with RTX as an add-on therapy consecutively seen at the MG Clinic of AziendaOspedaliero-UniversitariaPisanain order to investigate clinical predictors of better outcome after RTX infusion.
Of the 36 patients included, 27 were women (75%) and antibodies against muscle specific tyrosine kinase (MuSK) were present in 18 patients (50%); mean (SD) age at treatment start was 48(17) years. Mean (SD) MGFA composite score before RTX administration was 12,3 (8,3) and 3,5 (5,7) at last follow-up. 5 patients achieved complete stable remission and discontinued steroid therapy. We did not observe significant differences in clinical outcome and steroid sparing effectbetween the two MG subtypes, indeed patients who received RTX within 12 months of disease onset had a greater improvement in MGFA composite score in both groups (p < 0.001).
In our cohort of gMGpatients early administration of RTX (e.g. within 12 months of disease onset) obtained better outcomes in terms of clinical improvement irrespectively of antibody status, suggesting a greater benefit of RTX earlier in the disease course also in new-onset generalized MuSK-MG. Larger prospective studies are warranted to provide further evidence.

TammamG VegezziE GanaS PichiecchioA AlfonsiE CosentinoG 1 Department of Brain and Behavioral Sciences, University of Pavia, Pavia, Italy2

## Abstract
Mutations of SCN4A are associated with a wide spectrum of disorders of which non-dystrophic myotonias and periodic paralysis (PPs) represent the main clinical phenotypes. In the last years, atypical and overlapping phenotypes have been described. Here we present a patient carrying a heterozygous mutation in SCN4A who experienced acute rhabdomyolysis.
A 24-year-old Asian woman with uneventful past clinical history was admitted to the emergency department because of intense myalgia after moderate physical activity. Laboratory tests showed serum CK levels > 50.000 U/l and hypokalemia (2.4 mEq/l). Also, early fatigue and myalgia following gym exercise were recorded and CKs persisted mildly elevated after recovery. Both her mother and grandmother have been complaining of similar symptoms. Neurological examination was unremarkable except for mild bilateral calf hypertrophy. Both needle EMG, long exercise test and muscle MRI of the lower limbs proved normal. Clinical exome sequencing identified a novel heterozygous missense mutation in SCN4A, c.2147T > A (p.Val716Glu). This variant is absent from gnomAD genomes, it is predictive to be disruptive according to algorithms, and it segregates with the disease since it was detected also in the proband’s mother.
Rhabdomyolysis together with myalgia and fatigue can be a rare, although not negligible, clinical presentation of hypokalaemic-PP due to SCN4A mutations. Taken together, the patient’s clinical phenotype, although atypical, the family pedigree and segregation, strongly point to a causative role of the identified genetic variant. In conclusion, SCN4A mutations should be carefully considered in the differential diagnosis of rhabdomyolysis, even in the absence of overt PP.

## Abstract
The ACTN2 gene encodes the protein alpha-actinin-2 expressed in skeletal and cardiac muscle, the main component of the Z-disk where it plays a central role in “crosslinking” actin and titin filamentsVariants in ACTN2 have been firstly associated with cardiomyopathy and, in recent years, with an adult-onset form of distal myopathy named Adult-Onset Distal Myopathy 6 (MDP6; OMIM #618655) and a more severe condition named Congenital Myopathy with Structured Cores and Z-Line Abnormalities (Myocoz; MSCD; CMYP8 OMIM #618654).
A 48-years-old male presented with progressive distal lower limb and facial weakness since the age of 25. His mother had a scapulo-peroneal myopathy since the age of 40. They were diagnosed as an atypical FacioScapuloHumeral Muscular Dystrophy by a 32 kb D4Z4 fragment on chromosome 4Proband neurological examination showed limb muscle weakness and atrophy predominantly involving the distal segments associated to a moderate upper and lower facial muscle weaknessA neurophysiological, myopathological and imaging assessment was carried out. NGS analysis for distal myopathies and a familial segregation analysis were conductedCK, LDH and lactic acid were within normal limits. Electromyography showed diffuse myogenic changes more evident in the distal segments of the lower limbs. Cardiological examination was normalMultiple fibers with single central core-like abnormalities and rimmed vacuoles were observed on muscle biopsy. Muscle MRI showed severe atrophy and fat infiltration in the leg muscles with relative sparing of the soleus and gastrocnemius. NGS study detected the novel 2624_2625dup (Pro876Cysfster26) mutation in ACTN2 resulting in changing the reading frame with prediction of pathogenicityMyocoz is a congenital condition with facial weakness, cleft palate and diffuse muscle weakness and atrophy. Rimmed vacuoles and central cores are usually observed on muscle biopsyDifferently, MDP6 usually presents in adulthood (34-63 years) and is characterized by slowly progressive distal muscle weakness primarily affecting the lower limbs and resulting in gait difficulties, drooping foot and muscle atrophy. Facial, respiratory, and cardiac muscles were usually unaffected. Some patients may develop proximal and upper limb muscle weakness. Muscle biopsy may show increased numbers of internal nuclei, rimmed vacuoles and fibrotic or fatty replacement.
Our patient presents clinical and histopathological features of intermediate severity between Myocoz and MDP6, characterized by a predominantly distal involvement associated with facial weakness and central cores and rimmed vacuoles on muscle biopsyOur study suggest that ACTN2-related disease is a complex and more heterogeneous condition than previously believed. Phenotypes intermediate between the severe congenital and the adult distal form may be observed and easily misdiagnosed

## Abstract
Becker muscular dystrophy is caused by a partial defect of dystrophin. The clinical spectrum is broad, ranging from Duchenne-like to paucisymptomatic phenotypes, with ambulation retained until adulthood. The standard of care aims at managing symptoms, complications, and improving quality of life, however a disease modifying therapy is not available.
We present the case of a 8 year-old boy who was referred following an episode of acute pain and swelling in his calves after a football match. Despite normal stregth, severe hyperCKemia (32741 U/l) was detected and mild hyperlordosis and calf hypertrophy were noticeable. His family history was significant for childhood-onset “paraparesis” affecting two uncles on maternal side. A muscle biopsy was performed, showing marked fiber polydimensionality, central nuclei, lymphomonocytic infiltration accompanied by some increase in the perimisial connective tissue. Western blot and immunofluorescence showed regular weight dystrophin, but of reduced amount compared to the control. MLPA of DMD resulted negative and eventually the gene sequencing analysis identified a nonsense mutation in DMD. The patient is now 18 years old and presents with lower limbs’ mild proximal weakness with difficulty in running and climbing stairs. Moreover, he has developed dilated cardiomyopathy with impairment of left ventricular ejection fraction (37%).
This patient presents an intermediate Duchenne/Becker phenotype in the clinical spectrum of dystrophinopathies. Ataluren has been proven to be an effective therapy in nonsense mutations for DMD patients but is not currently approved for patients with measurable dystrophin expressed in muscle. This patient might potentially benefit from an off-label treatment with nonsense readthrough agents

## Abstract
We report the first case of a late-onset Congenital Myasthenic Syndrome (CMS) associated with a heterozygous missense variant in Prolyl-Endopeptidase-Like (PREPL) geneA 52-years-old male with clinical onset in the first decade with impaired ocular motility and diplopia. From third decade he developed bilateral eyelid ptosis, slowly progressive fatigability and weakness in the upper and lower limbs. Neurological examination showed bilateral ophthalmoparesis, shoulder-girdle hypotrophy and weakness, mild bilateral weakness of iliopsoas, rectus femoris and hamstring muscles and reduced triceps ROTs. Fatigability tests were positive. Normal CPK. Electrodiagnostic testing showed a pathological repetitive nerve stimulation and single fiber EMG findings, associated with a myopathic pattern on EMG. So, we performed dosage of Anti-AChR-Abs, anti-MuSK-Abs and anti-LRP4-Abs that resulted negative. Muscle MRI showed no significant changes. Muscle biopsy excluded a mitochondrial disease. However, we found slight myopathic changes (marked variability in size, nuclear centralizations, increase in connective tissue and a prevalence of type I fibers). Overall, we suspected a CMS and we started Pyridostigmine 240mg/die with benefit. To identify the genetic cause of the disease, we performed whole-exome sequencing and found a heterozygous missense variant in the PREPL gene, namely c.473T > C (p.Ile158Thr)PREPL deficiency (MIM# 616224) is a rare usually autosomal recessive inherited congenital myasthenic syndrome characterized by neonatal hypotonia, feeding problems, mild dysmorphism, neuromuscular symptoms and growth deficits. In almost all cases this syndrome is caused by biallelic deletion/duplication in the PREPL gene but recently. In our patient we found only one PREPL mutation, which might explain his clinical picture

Acta MyologicaActa MyolA case of TRIP4-associated Congenital Muscular Dystrophy: clinical and genetic findings
DecioA GiordaR CimaR MarelliS DiellaE SacchiD LegnaniC VillaF BassiMT D’AngeloMG 1Peripheral and Central Nervous System Rare Diseases Unit, IRCCS Medea, Bosisio Parini (LC), Italy1 Molecular Biology Laboratory, IRCCS Medea, Bosisio Parini (LC), Italy162023421 Suppl 14141–113©2023 Gaetano Conte Academy - Mediterranean Society of Myology, Naples, ItalyThis is an open access article distributed in accordance with the CC-BY-NC-ND (Creative Commons Attribution-NonCommercial-NoDerivatives 4.0 International) license. ## Abstract
TRIP4 gene (OMIM 60450) encodes for a member of a protein family of transcriptional coregulators with a crucial role in myotube growth. Homozygous mutations are associated to a recently discovered form of congenital muscular dystrophy called Davignon-Chauveau type whose core features are 1) severe neonatal hypotonia with respiratory failure 2) axial involvement with hypotonia, scoliosis or rigid spine 3) skin involvement with joint hyperlaxity.
We report the case of an 8 year-old girl born from non-consanguineous healthy parents both originating from a little town. At birth she presented as a floppy infant and suffered from respiratory insufficiency and feeding problems, requiring prolonged hospitalization and mechanical ventilation. In the following years, her neurological picture was characterized by global hypotonia and hyposthenia more marked in axial muscles, joint hyperlaxity, hyper elastic skin, pectus excavatum, scoliosis, intellectual impairment. She reached independent ambulation at 4 years but she can walk unassisted only for a few meters. She is mechanical ventilation dependent. At 6 years, she developed a dilatative cardiomyopathy requiring medical treatment. Electromyography showed myogenic changes. Muscle biopsy was not performed for parental choice. A NGS gene panel targeted at muscle disorders discovered a homozygous deletion in exons 10 and 11 of TRIP4 gene (confirmed by qPCR). Parents were heterozygous for the deletion.
The case is interesting as clinical findings are overlapping between congenital myopathies and congenital muscular dystrophies. The gene TRIP4 has been recently described and published cases are very few; it should be included in NGS gene panels for muscle disorders.

## Abstract
We describe the case of a child with a pre-natal diagnosis of 5q deletion, with three copies of SMN2.
Our patient has a syster affected by Spinal muscular atrophy type II who has been treated with Nusinersen since 5 years of age.
He was born at term after a normal pregnancy, with normal fetal movements and no perinatal problems. Neurological evaluation after birth showed cramp-like motility, mild reduction of osteo-tendinous reflexes and lingual fasciculations.
On electroneurography the ulnar and median nerve amplitudes were bilaterally reduced.
These elements made the child symptomatic and it was therefore possible to offer the parents the possibility of gene therapy with Zolgensma.
The parents chose the gene therapy option; the treatment was well tolerated and no adverse events have emerged more than a year later.
We would like to share this case as we have reached one year of clinical and electroneurographic follow-up and we have developed a number of doubts on how to organise the follow-up and how to motivate the family to continue the rehabilitation therapy.

Acta MyologicaActa MyolPrelimininary data on safety and efficacy of Risdiplam treatment in a small cohort of adult 5q spinal muscular atrophy
NanniAG MilellaG PiccirilliG UcciM IdrissiS FraddosioA IntronaA ScaccoV MegnaM SimoneI D’ErricoE 1Neurology Unit, Department of Translational Biomedicine and Neurosciences (DiBraiN), University of Bari1 Department of Translational Biomedicine and Neurosciences (DiBraiN), University of Bari, Bari, Italy162023421 Suppl 14141–113©2023 Gaetano Conte Academy - Mediterranean Society of Myology, Naples, ItalyThis is an open access article distributed in accordance with the CC-BY-NC-ND (Creative Commons Attribution-NonCommercial-NoDerivatives 4.0 International) license. For further information: https://creativecommons.org/licenses/by-nc-nd/4.0/deed.en

## Abstract
Risdiplam is an oral small-molecule drug recently approved for the treatment of Spinal Muscular Atrophy (SMA). It increases functional SMN (survival motor neuron) protein by modifying pre- mRNA splicing of the gene SMN2. Aim of the study was to investigate safety and efficacy of Risdiplam in our adult cohort of SMA patients.
Inclusion criteria were clinical and molecular diagnosis of SMA2/SMA3; starting Risdiplam in adulthood; availability of clinical data and specific motor scale [Hammersmith Functional Motor Scale Expanded (HFMSE); Revised Upper Limb Module (RULM), six minute walking test (6MWT)] at treatment baseline (T0) and after 6 months (T6).
We included 16 patients (10 SMA 2 and 6 SMA3), 41 years median age at first administration (IQR 21-54). HFMSE increased significantly from T0 to T6 (median values: 2, IQR: 0-13 vs 3, IQR: 0-15, p = 0.026). The RULM significantly improved from T0 to T6 (median values: 15, IQR: 6-21 vs16, IQR: 9-26, p = 0.024). No changes in 6MWT were detected at T6 in walking patients. Eleven patients (69%) were classified as responders at T6. Among all demographic and clinical variables, only the number of SMN2 copy was directly associated with clinical improvement (p = 0.023). The one mild adverse effect was reversible diarrhoea in 18% of patients, requiring drug discontinuation. No severe-adverse events were reported.
Our data highlight the efficacy of Risdiplam even in the first six months of treatment, regardless of age/gender/functional clinical status at baseline and SMA type. The number of SMN2 copies influences positively clinical improvement.

Acta MyologicaActa MyolProfile of cognitive abilities in spinal muscular atrophy type II and III
BuchignaniB CicalaG MoriconiF RicciM CapassoA CorattiG CasiraghiJ AlbamonteE CristofaniP CutronaC PeraMC AntonaciL RoncoroniC ChieffoD SansoneVA BattiniR PaneM MercuriE 1 Department of Developmental Neuroscience, IRCCS Stella Maris, Calambrone, Pisa, Italy2 Ph.D. Programme in Clinical and Translational Sciences, University of Pisa, Pisa, Italy3 Centro Clinico Nemo, Fondazione Policlinico Universitario Agostino Gemelli IRCCS, Rome4 Pediatric Neurology, Università Cattolica del Sacro Cuore, Rome, Italy5 Psychology Unit, Fondazione Policlinico Universitario Agostino Gemelli IRCCS, Rome6 Neurorehabilitation Unit, University of Milan, Centro Clinico Nemo, Niguarda Hospital, Milano, Italy7 Department of Clinical and Experimental Medicine, University of Pisa, Pisa, Italy* Contributed equally as co- first authors
162023421 Suppl 14141–113©2023 Gaetano Conte Academy - Mediterranean Society of Myology, Naples, ItalyThis is an open access article distributed in accordance with the CC-BY-NC-ND (Creative Commons Attribution-NonCommercial-NoDerivatives 4.0 International) license. ## Abstract
Cognitive impairment in Spinal Muscular Atrophy (SMA) is under discussion and evidence of a correlation with more severe phenotypes has emerged.
This study aims to systematically assess the cognitive profiles of individuals with SMA type II and III and to explore possible correlations with motor function abilities.
Individuals with a diagnosis of SMA type II or III, aged 3.5-17 years, were consecutively enrolled. Cognitive function was assessed using the Weschler Scales (WPPSI-III, WISC-IV). Motor function abilities were measured using disease-specific functional scales: Revised Upper Limb Module (RULM) and Hammersmith Functional Motor Scale Expanded (HFMSE).
38/57 patients had SMA type II, 19/57 had SMA type III. 72% had a normal IQ, 14% borderline intellectual functioning and 11% an above normal IQ, 3% had a mild intellectual disability. No difference was found in relation to SMA types, gender, or functional status. Subjects with SMA type II performed significantly better in working memory than subjects with SMA type III. Conversely, we found that Coding, Picture Concepts and Symbol Search subtests had significant positive correlations with Motor functional scores.
Intellectual disability is uncommon in SMA type II and III. However, this research shows an interesting relationship between motor function and cognitive profile.
Motor function was found to influence tasks requiring movement and speed, but the involvement of other abilities was not dependent on motor performance.
The peculiarity of the cognitive profiles in this population may suggest new insights in cortical reorganization and brain plasticity in patients with SMA.

Acta MyologicaActa MyolNusinersen in Children with Spinal Muscular Atrophy: Experience of a Pediatric Pain Center
AgostoC SalernoA BenedettiF MaghiniI DivisicA RusalenF ZaninA AvagninaA PerilongoG BeniniF 1 Pediatric Pain and Palliative Care Service, Department of Women’s and Children’s Health, University Hospital of Padua, Italy2
Pediatric Residency Program- University of Padua, Italy3 Pediatric Neurology, Department of Women’s and Children’s Health, University Hospital of Padua, Italy162023421 Suppl 14141–113©2023 Gaetano Conte Academy - Mediterranean Society of Myology, Naples, ItalyThis is an open access article distributed in accordance with the CC-BY-NC-ND (Creative Commons Attribution-NonCommercial-NoDerivatives 4.0 International) license. ## Abstract
The intrathecal administration in children requires anxiety and pain control and can be challenging due to severe scoliosis.
We aimed to present our clinical experience in procedural pain control in patients treated with nusinersen. Setting: tertiary academic children’s hospital; SMA patients are enrolled since diagnosis in the Pediatric Pain and Palliative Care (PPPC) Network which coordinates follow-up and therapeutic choices with neurologists. The administration takes place in PPPC Service with pharmacological (midazolam, ketamine and/or propofol, EMLAÒ patch) and non-pharmacological treatments according to internal protocol (sedation obtained to a Pediatric Sedation State Scale 1-2). Children who receive overnight non-invasive ventilation (NIV) are ventilated during the procedure. Children with severe scoliosis received CT-guided administration outside our department.
We conducted retrospective medical record review of children with genetically confirmed SMA (0-25 years) who received nusinersen (Feb 2018 - Dec 2022). We collected: demographics, SMA sub-types, total number of infusions, sedation-related adverse events (AEs).
During the study period 41 children (22 F; median age at first infusion 7,5 years) received a total of 353 infusions. Diagnoses: SMA1 18/41; SMA2 16/41; SMA 3 7/41. We experienced 5 sedation-related AEs: laryngospasm 1/353, hypotension 2/353, desaturation (SpO2 > 80%) 2/353. We collected non-sedation related symptoms in the 3 days following the procedure: back pain 11/353, headache 7/353, fatigue 2/353.
In our experience, we observed control of procedural pain and anxiety, high level of safety during procedure. The majority of symptoms collected were not clearly sedation related and remains unclear if procedure related.

VacchianoV MorabitoF FainiC NotR ScarpiniG PiniA LiguoriR 1
IRCCS Istituto delle Scienze Neurologiche di Bologna, Bologna, Italy2 Dipartimento di Scienze Biomediche e Neuromotorie, Università di Bologna, Bologna, Italy162023421 Suppl 14141–113©2023 Gaetano Conte Academy - Mediterranean Society of Myology, Naples, ItalyThis is an open access article distributed in accordance with the CC-BY-NC-ND (Creative Commons Attribution-NonCommercial-NoDerivatives 4.0 International) license. ## Abstract
CMAP Scan MUNE (Mscan) provides a novel tool for motor unit number estimation. Few studies explored its utility in spinal muscular atrophy (SMA) patients, only assessing the abductor brevis pollicis (ABP) muscle. Here we assessed ABP, abductor digiti minimi (ADM) and tibialis anterior (TA) muscles in pediatric and adult SMA patients, exploring clinical-electrophysiological correlations.
We analysed Mscan parameters which reflect the extent of denervation (MUNE = estimated number of functional motor units; N50 = estimated number of larger units making up 50-100% of CMAP amplitude) and parameters of collateral reinnervation. SMA patients were clinically examined using the revised version of Upper Limb Module (RULM) and the Hammersmith Functional Motor Scale – Expanded (HFMSE).
22 SMA patients (9 SMA2 and 13 SMA3) and 11 age-matched healthy controls (HC) were enrolled. SMA patients showed lower MUNE (p < 0.001) and N50 values (p < 0.001) and higher parameters of collateral sprouting (p < 0.001) in all muscles compared to HC. SMA2 patients presented lower MUNE values and higher values of collateral reinnervation in ABP and TA muscles compared to SMA3 (p < 0.05). Walker patients showed higher values of MUNE and N50, and lower parameters of reinnervation in all muscles compared to sitter patients (p < 0.05). MUNE values in the three muscles strongly correlated with RULM and HFMSE (best performances of ADM: Rho = 0.8, p < 0.001 for both scales).
Mscan well discriminated patients from controls, proving an impairment of distal muscles in SMA patients. Mscan parameters firmly correlated with functional status of patients. Strong clinical-neurophysiological correlations support Mscan use as a surrogate biomarker.

Acta MyologicaActa MyolLong-term comparative efficacy and safety of risdiplam versus nusinersen in children with Type 1 spinal muscular atrophy
HawkinsN SajeevG EvansR MahajanA ScottDA NamJ GakiE SutherlandCS KokaliarisC 1
Visible Analytics, Oxford, UK2
Institute of Health & Wellbeing, University of Glasgow, Glasgow, UK3

Global Access, F. Hoffmann-La Roche Ltd, Basel, Switzerland6
Roche Products Ltd, Welwyn Garden City, UK162023421 Suppl 14141–113©2023 Gaetano Conte Academy - Mediterranean Society of Myology, Naples, ItalyThis is an open access article distributed in accordance with the CC-BY-NC-ND (Creative Commons Attribution-NonCommercial-NoDerivatives 4.0 International) license. ## Abstract
There are no long-term data on relative efficacy or safety of risdiplam and nusinersen, approved therapies for spinal muscular atrophy (SMA), to inform treatment decision-making. In the absence of head-to-head trials, we conducted an adjusted indirect comparison of long-term efficacy and safety of risdiplam versus nusinersen in children with Type 1 SMA.
Patient-level risdiplam data from 58 children in FIREFISH (NCT02913482) were compared with published nusinersen data from 81 children in SHINE (ENDEAR cohort; NCT02193074). Populations were matched by age at first dose, disease duration and baseline Children’s Hospital of Philadelphia Infant Test of Neuromuscular Disorders (CHOP-INTEND) score. Hazard ratios were estimated for survival and the times to motor milestone response, CHOP-INTEND response, and serious adverse event (SAE). This was a non-randomized comparison.
After matching, relevant baseline characteristics were identical across groups. Median follow-up was 3 years (range 2.5–4.5). Compared with the nusinersen group, the risdiplam group had 78% lower rate of death (95% CI 53–96%), 81% lower rate of death or permanent ventilation (95% CI 65–93%), 57% lower rate of SAEs (95% CI 42–68%), and higher rates of HINE-2 and CHOP- INTEND response. While adjustments were made for known prognostic factors, as in any non-randomized comparison, results may be confounded by unobserved baseline differences between groups.
Risdiplam was associated with improved long-term survival, motor function and SAEs versus nusinersen in children with Type 1 SMA. Additional data sources should be consulted in the future to expand on these findings.

## Abstract
Spinal Muscular Atrophy (SMA) is rarely caused by point mutations in the SMN1 gene.
We analyzed the phenotypic spectrum of three unrelated SMA patients with missense mutation (exon 7 c.840C > T) in SMN1 which causes its conversion to SMN2.
For the genetic analysis we used (MLPA) and (NGS) while for the clinical evaluation the (HFMSE) and the (RULM).
In our cohort twenty-four SMA patients we found three patients (2M/1F) with only one the deletion of exons 7 and 8 of SMN1 and a rare missense mutation (exon 7 c.840C > T). All patients had 2 copies of SMN2. In addition, one of two male patients with large deletion in NAIP gene, shows the most severe phenotype (SMA II) and at last neurological examination his score at HFMSE is 14 and RULM is 17. The other two patients were classified as SMA III, but their clinical severity was very different: the male’s scores was 18 at the HFMSE and RULM was 24 while the female patient showed a milder phenotype and presents HFMSE score 60 and RULM 38.
The rare point mutation that converts SMN1 to SMN2 seems to occur more frequently than described previously. Genotype-phenotype correlation analysis reveals a wide phenotypic variability among the patients that carry the conversion and clinical severity could be influenced by a gender effect and further studies are needed to evaluate this phenomenon. Mutations in other genes, such as NAIP deletions, could affect clinical severity in addition to SMN2 copies.

## Abstract
Distal spinal muscular atrophy (dSMA) is a rare genetically and clinically heterogeneous group of disorders characterized by slowly progressive distal muscular weakness and atrophy without sensory abnormalities. To date, at least 20 genes and 4 loci associated with dSMA have been reported.
We report the case of an Italian family with autosomal-dominant dSMA with eight affected members. Abnormal ambulation, due to weakness and atrophy in distal lower limbs, was the first sign in all patients. The same pattern of distal weakness and atrophy involved upper limbs later in disease course. Electromyography was neurogenic in distal muscles with reduced compound muscle action potential (CMAP) amplitudes. Sensory conduction was normal. Muscle MRI in III-(2) and III-(3) documented muscle atrophy of legs and forearms with fibrous-adipose replacement. Three patients (II-(3), III-(2), III-(3)) were also affected by sensorineural hearing loss confirmed by audiometry. To identify the causative variant in this family, we completed an exome sequencing of 136 genes in all affected members still alive, II-(3), III-(2), III-(3), III-(4), IV-(1) and IV-(2). Genetic analysis identified in all of them the heterozygous c.291G > T variant in gene MYH14, which was classified as likely pathogenic by genome aggregation database (gnomAD).
Although variants in MYH14 gene are yet known to be associated with progressive dSMA, the direct correlation between clinical phenotype and the c.291G > T variant confirms the pathogenicity of this mutation in dSMA. Our findings broaden the genetic spectrum of MYH14 associated dSMA and could improve molecular diagnostics of dSMA.

Acta MyologicaActa MyolNew challenges in the treatment era of Spinal muscular atrophy. A single centre experience with risdiplam
DosiC MinacapilliE ZaninR ArnoldiMT MassonR 1Developmental Neurology Unit, Department of Pediatric Neurosciences, Fondazione IRCCS Istituto Neurologico Carlo Besta, Milan, Italy162023421 Suppl 14141–113©2023 Gaetano Conte Academy - Mediterranean Society of Myology, Naples, ItalyThis is an open access article distributed in accordance with the CC-BY-NC-ND (Creative Commons Attribution-NonCommercial-NoDerivatives 4.0 International) license. ## Abstract
The arrival of new therapies has produced a significant change in the natural history of spinal muscular atrophy (SMA). In recent years, three disease-modifying drugs have been approved, nusinersen, onasemnogene aberparvovec and risdiplam, the latest approved.
The use of these medications in combination is not allowed in Italy, beyond the clinical trials. Consequently, at the time of the study, no patient received concurrent treatment with at least two available drugs.
The clinical decision regarding the opportunity of switching from one drug to another (e.g. from nusinersen to risdiplam) and the timing of administration of a second therapy is always challenging. Currently, there are no published guidelines or clinical recommendations regarding the switch of therapies, which remains a decision of the clinician and the patient/family.
Herein, we report 40 SMA patients currently treated with risdiplam at the Developmental Neurology Unit, Fondazione IRCCS Istituto Neurologico Carlo Besta. We analyzed the clinical features of the patients, regarding SMA form and motor abilities; the different pharmacological history of the patients (e.g. naïve vs switching treatments); the therapeutic adherence to risdiplam schedule.
All 40 patients are in regular follow up and, based on the type of SMA, they can be divided in: 12 (30.7%) with a diagnosis of SMA 1, 20 (51.4%) SMA 2 and 6 (15.4%) SMA 3. Of interest, 1 patient (2.5%) was a presymptomatic child, diagnosed via newborn screening.
Almost half (n = 19) of the patients were previously enrolled in clinical trials of risdiplam, 13 patients (33.3%) were switched from nusinersen to risdiplam. All the others are naïve patients.
According with risdiplam protocol, the follow up includes a comprehensive evaluation with blood exams and neuromotor assessment every 6 months, with a weight control every 2 months for patients weighing less than 20 kg.
All the families except one have joined the home delivery program provided by the Centre, that allow them to receive the drug at home.
All the patients demonstrated good adherence to the schedule, none discontinued drug treatment, all patients complied with the follow-up program.

## Abstract
Advances in treatment over the past decade had significantly modify the natural course of Spinal Muscular Atrophy (SMA). As a consequences, new phenotypes are emerging and this aspect poses additional challenges in specific clinical care and rehabilitation.
We conducted a combined retrospective and prospective study on three patients with type 1 SMA treated with Nusinersen in order to describe motor function evolution from the point of view of the rehabilitation team. The patients differed from: SMN2 copies (3,2 and 2), age of presentation (6,3 and 7 months), Dubowitz classification (1.9, 1.5 and 1.9) and time of first administration of Nusinersen (10, 9 and 14 months of age). The aim was to collect new findings closely linked to capability in real world taking into account the functional motor scales (CHOP-INTEND, HFMSE). The rehabilitation project was developed to support emerging functions and prevent contractures and deformities, and was characterized by repeated functional evaluations, physical therapy and by the introduction of orthoses and mobility aids suchas seating system, spinal orthoses, verticalization using ankle-foot-orthose, standing and Salera hip-knee-ankle-foot orthosis to stand and than to walk using a rollator.
We observed that the turning points of the rehabilitation program filled in with the single items and total scores of the CHOP and HFMSE. We think that the integration of data from the rehabilitation project with those from functional scales helps to clarify the new phenotypes of disease in SMA children treated with innovative therapies.

Acta MyologicaActa MyolUsing ICF-based functional profile to guide development of new measures for identifying treatment effects in SMA patients receiving disease-modifying therapies
OlivaMC FerranteC GalloI VespinoT SantoroA ManiglioI BarracoG DistanteF TrabaccaA 1Scientific Institute IRCCS “E. Medea”- Unit for Severe disabilities in developmental age and young adults (Developmental Neurology and Neurorehabilitation) - Brindisi – Italy162023421 Suppl 14141–113©2023 Gaetano Conte Academy - Mediterranean Society of Myology, Naples, ItalyThis is an open access article distributed in accordance with the CC-BY-NC-ND (Creative Commons Attribution-NonCommercial-NoDerivatives 4.0 International) license. ## Abstract
The advent of gene therapies has changed the natural history of SMA, making it necessary to identify the characteristics of the new phenotypes including the effects of treatment at different ages and stages of disease and long-term outcomes.
Nine treated patients were involved: three with SMA 1, three with SMA 2 (one no sitter), and three with SMA 3. CHOP Intend, HFMSE, RULM, 6-minute walking test, PedsQL, and ICF-CY were periodically assessed. SMA 1 patients (therapy introduced at mean age of 7 years (± 3,5)) and two of SMA 2 patients (therapy started at 14 and 7 years) showed no significative improvement in motor functions. Non-sitters variably reported an increased tone of voice, environmental participation, facial expression and resistance to the lack of assisted ventilation at ICF-CY. One patient with SMA 2 (therapy started at two years) and those with SMA 3 (especially the one with smaller initial value) showed a significant increase in HFMSE values. Walkers and two sitters reported an improvement in the quality of life due to fatigue’s reduction at PEDsQL although only one showed an increase on 6MWT. Scoliosis and painful hips limit response to therapies.
ICF-CY is a valid tool for investigating the response to therapy, especially in more severe patients, and identifying any factors that may influence it. More specific measures are necessary to capture the functional improvements reported by patients or their parents, still un-detected by the current assessment scales, in order to notice improvements in SMA-related QoL for children and their parents.

## Abstract
Spinal Muscular Atrophy (SMA) is an inherited neuromuscular disease affecting the lower motor neuron.
With the new therapeutic options, sensitive clinical evaluation and outcome measures are needed, especially in adult patients, in which the expected clinical response is mostly stabilization of disease progression. We present preliminary data from the application of the EnduSMA protocol.
The total cohort includes 25 SMA 2-3 adult patients (14 M, 11 F). The clinical evaluations were scheduled every 4 months for a total of 12 months. We present data from clinical follow up of 15 patients. Moreover, wearable devices integrated evaluations have been tested on 2 patients with surface EMG, accelerometers and protractor.
Preliminary data from 9 patients evaluated in Pisa up to T8 show that the new scale is more sensitive to detect changes in motor performance in comparison with the standard scales (RULM, HFMSE).
The evaluation of endurance could represent a useful parameter that can integrate the standard clinical monitoring in the management and follow-up of SMA adult patients. Fatigability is a feature of SMA that can be determined with continuous repetitive tasks offering a quite objective measure of response to treatment. To integrate the fatigability evaluation as trial readiness in clinical trials should be reasonable, to implement a set of suitable biomarkers or clinical outcome measures which can fill up the gaps in our understanding of disease course.

Acta MyologicaActa MyolA potential biomarker in Spinal Muscular Atrophy: serum creatinine in nusinersen treated patients
PlacidiG DosiC ZaninR CiusaniE MassonR 1 Developmental Neurology Unit, Department of Pediatric Neurosciences, Fondazione IRCCS Istituto Neurologico Carlo Besta, Milan, Italy2 Laboratory of Neurological Biochemistry and Neuropharmacology, Fondazione IRCCS Istituto Neurologico Carlo Besta, Milan, Italy162023421 Suppl 14141–113©2023 Gaetano Conte Academy - Mediterranean Society of Myology, Naples, ItalyThis is an open access article distributed in accordance with the CC-BY-NC-ND (Creative Commons Attribution-NonCommercial-NoDerivatives 4.0 International) license. For further information: https://creativecommons.org/licenses/by-nc-nd/4.0/deed.en

## Abstract
Spinal Muscular Atrophy (SMA) is a degenerative motor neuron disorder, caused by homozygous deletion of SMN1 gene, characterized by progressive muscle atrophy and weakness leading to loss of movement. Biochemical investigations are commonly performed in SMA patients, mainly to monitor potential drugs-related adverse events. Moreover, it is urgent to identify biomarkers to understand disease progression, to detect therapeutic response and the variability of individual response among treated patients, with the goal of improving the clinical management of the disease.
Serum creatinine (SCr) is a waste product from skeletal muscle creatine metabolism and has proven to be a reliable biomarker of muscle mass change, and it is usually dosed in clinical practice to monitor renal function in treated patients. Recently, several studies have reported that patients with SMA have lower SCr concentrations than age-matched healthy controls; moreover, it has been suggested that SCr reflects disease severity in children and adolescents. However, the potential role of SCr as a biomarker of muscle denervation and motor function, especially in treated SMA patients, is still poorly investigated.
To test the hypothesis that SCr could be a reliable biomarker for monitoring progressive muscle atrophy or muscle sparing in patients with SMA, we determined whether SCr concentration correlates with disease progression and severity in a cohort of treated patients. We retrospectively reviewed the SCr concentrations of 48 patients with SMA who were treated with nusinersen in our Center. We found that, differently from the natural history, SCr levels remain stable in almost all the treated patients over years and correlate with the form of disease, with higher levels in type 3 SMA. Our results suggest that SCr should be monitored routinely in longitudinal follow-up of all SMA patients, being a potential biomarker of patients with unexpected worsening or poor responders.

Acta MyologicaActa MyolA case of SMA type 0: clinical findings and literature review
CapelliM ScarpiniG LaganàV GiannottaM LandolinaL SarajlijaJ DonatiI GrazianoC AncoraG CordelliD PiniA 1
UO TIN e Neonatologia – Ospedale Infermi di Rimini, Rimini Italia2

## Abstract
We report a severe rare case of Spinal Muscular Atrophy (SMA) type 0. The patient was the first baby female born at term, from unrelated parents. The pregnancy was complicated by increased NT, fetal hydrop at 13 weeks and reduced active fetal movements ten days before delivery. At the birth she was a SGA (Small for Gestational Age) with a weight of 2700 gr.
Soon after the birth the baby showed severe hypotonia and weakness, arthrogryposis with contractures of upper limbs and knees, absent deep tendon reflexes, poor spontaneous limb activity, tremor, hypomimia with open mouth and tongue fasciculations, sucking and swallowing deficits and preserved eye movements. Severe respiratory failure occurred so she needed mechanical ventilation, circulatory support, parenteral nutrition. The clinical picture was suggestive of a severe prenatal neuromuscular disorder and MLPA analysis of gene SMN1 confirmed the diagnosis of SMA type 0 with 1 copy of SMN2.
As reported in the literature, in addition to prenatal and neonatal clinical signs, in this type 0 SMA we found multi-systemic involvement: decreased cardiac contractility, ventricular hypertrophy and multiples DIVs documented by echocardiography and systemic hypertension with high levels of renina and aldosterone.
It was decided, in agreement with the family, to provide only compassionate care and baby died at 41 days of life.
The decision was based on the severity of the clinical picture with early cardiac involvement and on literature data showing no significant changes in natural history leading to early death in the few cases treated with Nusinersen or Onasemnogene abeparvovec.

## Abstract
Our study aimed at improving the treatment of Spinal Muscular Atrophy (SMA) by investigating the efficacy of a Risdiplam-tool compound on 3-dimensional (3D) spinal cord models. SMA is a severe neurological disorder characterized by early onset and degeneration of lower motor neurons due to mutations in the SMN1 gene. To reproduce reliable human models, we generate and phenotypically characterize human SMA type 1 spinal cord organoids from induced pluripotent stem cells (iPSCs) of n = 3 subjects.
Our analysis revealed that SMA presents a pervasive cellular and molecular developmental alteration in multiple cell populations, including neural progenitors, beyond motor neurons. We used omics approaches, including single cells, bulk transcriptomics, and multi-electrodes array analysis, along with immunophenotypic characterization. Treatment was started at different time points during the first 80 days of organoid development which parallels the first trimester post conception and was provided as daily therapy.
Our preliminary results demonstrated that Risdiplam-tool compound models ~ 15% of disease affected genes and long-term in vitro treatment is well-tolerated, supporting the idea that SMA organoids represent a reliable model to explore drug kinetics and efficacy. Our study highlights the early-onset and pervasive developmental nature of SMA pathogenesis, which should be considered in therapeutic perspectives. Optimizing Risdiplam-tool compound therapy for all SMA patients and developing combined therapeutic approaches can be achieved by understanding the complex mechanisms of the drug’s action.
Overall, our study contributes to the optimization of Risdiplam tool compound therapy and to the identification of complementary treatment target approaches for SMA patients.

## Abstract
SUNFISH (NCT02908685) is a multicenter, two-part, randomized, placebo-controlled, double-blind study in patients with Types 2 and 3 SMA (inclusion criteria: aged 2–25 years at enrollment). Part 1 assessed the safety, tolerability and pharmacokinetics/pharmacodynamics of different risdiplam dose levels in patients with Types 2 and 3 SMA. Part 2 assessed the efficacy and safety of the Part 1-selected dose of risdiplam versus placebo in Type 2 and non-ambulant Type 3 SMA for 12 months; participants then received risdiplam in a blinded manner until Month 24. At Month 24, patients were offered the open-label extension phase participation.
To determine the efficacy and safety of risdiplam in patients with Types 2 and 3 SMA after 4 years of treatment.
The primary endpoint (Part 2) of change from baseline in the 32-item Motor Function Measure (MFM32) total score in patients treated with risdiplam versus placebo was met at Month 12. These increases in motor function were sustained in the second and third year after risdiplam treatment, as measured by changes in the MFM32, and other scales. At Month 36, there were no treatment-related safety findings leading to withdrawal from either SUNFISH Part 1 or 2. Here we present 4-year efficacy and safety data.
SUNFISH is ongoing and will provide further long-term efficacy and safety data of risdiplam in a broad population of children, teenagers and adults with SMA.

ZoppiD BencivengaRP TozzaS RussoA NevanoA IodiceR DubbiosoR AcetoG ManganelliF RuggieroL 1Department of Neurosciences, Reproductive Sciences, and Odontostomatology, University of Naples Federico II, Naples, Italy162023421 Suppl 14141–113©2023 Gaetano Conte Academy - Mediterranean Society of Myology, Naples, ItalyThis is an open access article distributed in accordance with the CC-BY-NC-ND (Creative Commons Attribution-NonCommercial-NoDerivatives 4.0 International) license. ## Abstract
Retrospectively investigate efficacy of Risdiplam in a small cohort of Italian adult type 2 and non-ambulant type 3 Spinal Muscular Atrophy patients.
Inclusion criteria: clinical data available at the first evaluation (T0 – beginning of administration) and at the last evaluation carried out after a period of at least 12 months (range 12-24 months). HFMSE, RULM and MFM32 rating scales were administered.
We included 6 patients (5 SMA2 and 1 SMA3) with median age at first administration of 40 years (range 22-55) and an SMN2 copy number of 3 (4 patients) or 4 (2 patients). HFMSE in patient with SMA3 increased from baseline to last evaluation (+3 points). RULM in SMA3 also improved between T0 and last evaluation (+3 points). Conversely, patients with SMA2 had no significant changes, hence a steady state, of median HFMSE and RULM between T0 and the following time points (0 and +0.6 respectively), although an improvement of MFM32 was observed (median change +5.2 points). Furthermore, all patients report an at least subjective benefit especially with bulbar functions including respiratory function, tone of voice (reduction of rhinolalia) and head control. No adverse reactions or drug intolerance were recorded.
In conclusion, HFMSE and RULM showed an increase in scores for the SMA3 patient while in SMA2 the scores appear stationary. Therefore, in SMA2, among the currently used scales, MFM32 seems to be the most sensitive in detecting improvement. However, further tools seem to be needful to investigate bulbar functions.

Acta MyologicaActa MyolSerum MyomiRs: potential biomarkers for adult SMA patients upon nusinersen treatment
MalacarneC SaracenoF ZaninR AndreettaF MantegazzaR MaggiL MarcuzzoS BonannoS 1 Neurology IV, Neuroimmunology and Neuromuscular Diseases Unit, Fondazione IRCCS Istituto Neurologico Carlo Besta, Milan, Italy2
University of Parma, Department of Chemistry, Life Sciences and Environmental Sustainability, Parma, Italy3 Developmental Neurology Unit, Fondazione IRCCS Istituto Neurologico Carlo Besta, Milan, Italy162023421 Suppl 14141–113©2023 Gaetano Conte Academy - Mediterranean Society of Myology, Naples, ItalyThis is an open access article distributed in accordance with the CC-BY-NC-ND (Creative Commons Attribution-NonCommercial-NoDerivatives 4.0 International) license. The article can be used by giving appropriate credit and mentioning the license, but only for non-commercial purposes and only in the original version. For further information: https://creativecommons.org/licenses/by-nc-nd/4.0/deed.en

## Abstract
Spinal muscular atrophy (SMA) is an autosomal recessive disorder caused by mutations in survival motor neuron (SMN) 1 gene, resulting in a truncated SMN protein responsible for degeneration of brain stem and spinal motor neurons. The paralogous SMN2 gene partially compensates full-length SMN protein production, mitigating the phenotype. Antisense oligonucleotide nusinersen (Spinraza®) enhances SMN2 gene expression. SMN is involved in RNA metabolism and biogenesis of microRNA (miRNA), crucial gene expression modulators, whose dysregulation contributes to motor neuron diseases. Being stable in body fluids and reflecting distinct pathophysiological states, miRNAs are emerging mirrors of therapy outcomes in pathological conditions. However, their potential as biomarkers for clinical use in adult SMA patients has not been investigated yet. Here we explored the expression levels of the circulating muscle-specific microRNAs (myomiRs) in serum of 16 adult SMA patients at baseline, after 6 and 14 months of nusinersen treatment, and in age-matched healthy controls. Our findings demonstrated an upregulation of miR-206, miR-133a and miR-133b in serum of adult SMA patients compared to controls before treatment (p < 0,001, p < 0,01 and p < 0,05 respectively). Notably, after 14 months of nusinersen, their expression levels decreased till normalization (p < 0,001, p < 0,05, p < 0,001 respectively). Further, miR-133a levels at baseline positively correlated with response to therapy evaluated by the Hammersmith Functional Motor Scale Expanded (HFMSE), corroborating our previous findings in pediatric patients. Our work underlines myomiRs as potential biomarkers to monitor disease progression and therapeutic response in adult SMA patients.

## Abstract
We collect and analyze assistance requests from Local Hospital Pharmacies (LHPs) which directly deliver risdiplam to families.
The Paediatric Palliative Care (PPC) physicians prescribe the drug, pharmacists of the Department of Hospital Pharmacy (DP) reconstitute it. First administration and supply take place at the PPC Centre: both physician and pharmacist discuss with the family and the patient how to administer and store the drug. Following deliveries are dealt by LHPs: to assist in the correct preparation and delivery of the drug, the PPC physician and the DP pharmacist assisted the families and LHPs 24/7.
Prospective collection of assistance requests from LHPs (March 2021-March 2023) at t0, t2, t6, t8 months. Other data collected: demographics, prescription reasons, treatment adherence.
Fourteen patients (F:7) were prescribed risdiplam, median age at eligibility: 14 yrs (4yrs-24yrs). All patients were already being treated with nusinersen and shifted to risdiplam for the following reasons: 12 for severe scoliosis, 2 for parents’ decisions. Patients referred to 9 different LHPs. Assistance from LHPs to the hubs: t0 average of 4 calls for each patient; t8 average of 0 calls for each patient. Treatment adherence at home was 100% (14/14).
The multidisciplinary team of PPC physicians and DP pharmacists contributed to an adequate supplementation of drugs from LHPs, avoiding the need for families to travel and increasing adherence to treatment.

## Abstract
Risdiplam is an orally administered drug targeting SMN2 gene with a systemic approach. The initial entry criteria to prescribe risdiplam prior to the extended commercial use of the drug have changed over the last 3 years and the cohorts who have been placed on treatment are very heterogeneous. This may represent a challenge when looking at tolerability, adherence and efficacy.
We describe the clinical and demographic features of patients with SMA on treatment with risdiplam amongst the Italian centers included in the Italian SMA Consortium (ItaSMAc) included in the Compassionate Use Program (CUP) and then when the drug became commercially available in 2022.
We retrospectively reviewed charts from patients with SMA included in the CUP (cohort 1) and compared clinical and demographic features as well as neuromotor, respiratory, nutritional data at baseline from patients treated since 2022 when risdiplam became commercially available (cohort 2). 6-12-month follow-up data when available were also collected.
Preliminary results from 120 patients from cohorts 1 (CUP, mean age 26 years ± 13) and from 120 patients accessing risdiplam commercially (cohort 2, mean age 26 years ± 18) show that patients in Cohort 1 are mostly type 2, non sitters. Patients in cohort 2 are mostly types 3 and types 2, with the majority being sitters (69 of 120) and some standers (n = 3) and walkers (n = 21). Disability is more severe in cohort 1 (90% on NIV, 10 % on tracheostomy ; 3% GI tubes, 27 % PEG tube). 20% in both cohorts were shifts from nusinersen. Our data confirm the safety profile of risdiplam.
The clinical and demographic features of patients in treatment with risdiplam is very different between the two cohorts. This has potential implication on efficacy and long-term data. Currently this is the broadest experience of the use of risdiplam in patients with SMA in Italy.

RicciG FontanelliL ChiappiniR TorriF GherardiM SicilianoG 1Neurology Unit, Department of Clinical and Experimental Medicine, University of Pisa162023421 Suppl 14141–113©2023 Gaetano Conte Academy - Mediterranean Society of Myology, Naples, ItalyThis is an open access article distributed in accordance with the CC-BY-NC-ND (Creative Commons Attribution-NonCommercial-NoDerivatives 4.0 International) license.
