## Abstract
Many female patients reach fertile age and consider getting pregnant. However, only little information is available about outcomes and complications, influence of pregnancy on the disease course and therapy management. A decline in muscle strength possibly due to pregnancy itself and the stop of treatment may exacerbate disease burden. Further clinical evidences should be collect to better define pregnancy effects and risk-benefit of stopping treatment.
We report a case of a 33 year-old-woman affected with SMA type 3 (3 copies of SMN2) walker, in treatment with Nusinersen since 2018 who decided to stop treatment in 2021 in order to plan a pregnancy. Her daughter was born in February 2022. Delivery, at full term, was performed by caesarean section under general anesthesia, without any complications. She stopped therapy six months before conception. Since patient started therapy, her clinical conditions appear stable (HFMSE = 48, RULM = 27). Neurological examination performed one month after delivery shown a decline of 21 points in Hammersmith Functional Motor Scale (HFMSE = 27) and 6 points in Revised Upper Limb Module (RULM = 21) scale. In details, a marked decline in the strength of proximal muscles were noted as she became unable to climb off and descend stairs, and to initiate the squat and hold the head up on hands and knee. Subjectively, the patient reports a worsening in terms of fatigue in daily activities. She also began to feel lower back pain (LBP) without signs of radiculopaty.

## Abstract
Risdiplam is a centrally and peripherally distributed, oral therapy approved for the treatment of Spinal muscular atrophy (SMA).
JEWELFISH (NCT03032172) is a multicenter, open-label study of risdiplam in patients with SMA (inclusion criteria: aged 6 months–60) who were previously enrolled in the MOONFISH study (RG7800) or previously treated with nusinersen, olesoxime or onasemnogene abeparvovec.
To determine the safety, tolerability, pharmacokinetics and pharmacodynamics (PD) of risdiplam in non-treatment-naïve SMA patients.
The enrolled population (N = 174) included a broad range of ages (1–60 years), SMA types, SMN2 copy numbers and motor functions (non-sitters/sitters/walkers). Of the 174 patients enrolled, 13 patients were previously enrolled in MOONFISH, 76 received nusinersen, 70 received olesoxime and 14 received onasemnogene abeparvovec. One patient withdrew from the study at baseline. Risdiplam treatment led to a > 2-fold increase in SMN protein versus baseline within 4 weeks, irrespective of previous treatment. No drug-related safety findings leading to withdrawal were reported. The safety profile of risdiplam was consistent with the safety profile in treatment-naïve patients observed in other studies. Based on the exploratory efficacy analysis, an overall stabilization of motor function was observed as assessed by the 32-item Motor Function Measure and Revised Upper Limb Module scales.
JEWELFISH is ongoing at sites across Europe and the USA and is providing important data on the safety, PD and exploratory efficacy of risdiplam in a broad population of non-treatment-naïve patients with SMA.

Acta MyologicaActa MyolAttenuated total reflection-Fourier transform infrared spectroscopy (ATR-FTIR) in muscle diseases
PrimianoG SancriccaC PrimianoA GervasoniJ UrbaniA MariniF SabinoA CalvaniR ServideiS 1 Dipartimento di Neuroscienze, Organi di Senso e Torace, Fondazione Policlinico Universitario Agostino Gemelli IRCCS, Rome, Italy2 Dipartimento di scienze di laboratorio e infettivologiche, Fondazione Policlinico Universitario Agostino Gemelli IRCCS, Rome, Italy3 Department of Chemistry, Sapienza University of Rome, Rome, Italy4 Dipartimento di scienze dell’Invecchiamento, Ortopediche e Reumatologiche, Fondazione Policlinico Universitario Agostino Gemelli IRCCS, Rome, Italy162023421 Suppl 14141–113©2023 Gaetano Conte Academy - Mediterranean Society of Myology, Naples, ItalyThis is an open access article distributed in accordance with the CC-BY-NC-ND (Creative Commons Attribution-NonCommercial-NoDerivatives 4.0 International) license. ## Abstract
Inherited muscle diseases are a heterogeneous group of clinical conditions, characterized by histological and functional abnormalities of skeletal muscle. Attenuated Total Reflectance (ATR) is one of the sampling technologies used for infrared spectroscopy and, as a rapid and non-destructive technique, it is increasingly used in different biological applications. The aim of this study was to evaluate whether the biochemical profile determined by the ATR-Fourier transform infrared (FTIR) spectroscopic technique would allow to distinguish patients affected by late-onset Pompe disease (LOPD), Becker muscular dystrophy (BMD), limb-girdle muscular dystrophies (LGMD), and healthy subjects (HS).
A total of 40 participants were included: 11 LOPD, 10 BMD, 10 LGMD, 9 HS.
For ATR-FTIR, muscle samples were cut at 10 μm in cross-section and placed onto diamond/ZnSe crystal for spectral analysis.
The results obtained show that the spectroscopic fingerprint embeds sufficient information to allow a correct classification of the majority of participants in three groups: dystrophic (BMD and LGMD) and metabolic (LOPD) myopathies, healthy subjects (accuracy 88.4 ± 7.1%). The ATR-FTIR analysis was also effective in classification rates using a two-class model: LOPD vs LGMD (accuracy 95.7 ± 3.2%), LOPD vs BMD (accuracy 82.9 ± 4.6%) and LOPD vs BMD+LGMD (accuracy 93.4 ± 3.0%).
Our data suggest that ATR-FTIR profile is a reliable diagnostic biomarker for LOPD, BMD and LGMD. Future directions will include evaluating its role as a prognostic biomarker in these genetic diseases, also analyzing biofluids, and the ability of this technique to shed light on the underlying pathogenic mechanisms.

FerreroA GironellaN ReF UbaldiA CocciaM 1 NeuroMuscular Omnicentre (NeMO) Ancona, Ospedali Riuniti di Torrette Ancona, Ancona, Italy162023421 Suppl 14141–113©2023 Gaetano Conte Academy - Mediterranean Society of Myology, Naples, ItalyThis is an open access article distributed in accordance with the CC-BY-NC-ND (Creative Commons Attribution-NonCommercial-NoDerivatives 4.0 International) license. ## Abstract
M. is a child affected by spinal muscular atrophy with respiratory distress type 1 (SMARD1), characterized by severe early onset respiratory involvement with permanent invasive ventilator support, complete paralysis of the four limbs and need for enteral feeding. He also has no verbal skills and a diagnosis of severe cognitive impairment. He came to our attention for the first time at the age of 7. Some attempts had already been made to improve his communication skills; he should have tried to get the caregiver’s attention by pressing down a microlight switch but the functional motor level did not allow an effective use of this device. Oculomotor competence appeared as a resource which could have been improved through the use of an eye-tracking communication device. The child, at first, learned to play simple cause-and-effect games. Initially, this allowed him to improve the visual exploration, to dissociate eye movements from head movements and to increase eye-fixation duration. Afterwards, he learned to activate a button with Augmentative and Alternative Communication (AAC) symbols, in order to make simple requests (continue a pleasure activity that was previously interrupted), becoming able to use it in different contexts (videos, stories, games etc.). In conclusion, the use of eye tracking technology allowed to increase his social participation that previously appeared to be not improvable. At the same time, it allowed to assess simple cognitive skills that are not evaluable with traditional instruments and that the child was not able to show before.

Acta MyologicaActa MyolSympathetic neuropathology is revealed in muscles affected by Amyotrophic Lateral Sclerosis
MazzaroA VitaV RonfiniM CasolaI KleinA DobrowolnyG SorarùG MusaròA MongilloM ZagliaT 1 Department of Cardiac, Thoracic, Vascular Sciences and Public Health, University of Padova, Italy2 Department of Biomedical Sciences, University of Padova, Italy3

162023421 Suppl 14141–113©2023 Gaetano Conte Academy - Mediterranean Society of Myology, Naples, ItalyThis is an open access article distributed in accordance with the CC-BY-NC-ND (Creative Commons Attribution-NonCommercial-NoDerivatives 4.0 International) license. ## Abstract
Althoguh the existence of muscle innervating sympathetic neurons (SNs) has been described for a long time, the structural and functional features of muscle sympathetic innervation remain largely undetermined, in both physiology and pathology. Amyotrophic Lateral Sclerosis (ALS) is a fatal neuromuscular disease mainly targeting motor neurons, and despite autonomic symptoms have been reported in patients, peripheral SNs are generally considered unaffected and, as such, poorly studied. On these bases, we here compared sympathetic innervation of normal and ALS muscles, in human and murine tissue samples.
Firstly, we refined tissue processing to circumvent methodologic limitations interfering with detection of small neuronal fibers. The optimized ‘Neuro Detection Protocol’ was validated in murine and human muscle biopsies, demonstrating that SNs innervate, at high density, both blood vessels and skeletal myofibers, independently from the fiber metabolic type. Subsequently, we analyzed muscles from SOD1G93A mice, a preclinical ALS model, and showed that they display sympathetic denervation, which starts already at the early disease stage and worsens during ageing. SN degeneration was also observed in muscles from mice with muscle-restricted expression of the SOD1G93A mutant gene (MLC/SOD1G93A mice). Notably, similar alterations in SNs were observed in muscle biopsies from an ALS patient, carrying the SOD1G93A mutation.
We implemented a protocol for the analysis of murine and, more importantly, human muscle sympathetic innervation. Our results indicate that SNs are additional cell types compromised in ALS and suggest that dysfunctional SOD1G93A muscles impact on their sympathetic innervation.

Acta MyologicaActa MyolA new 6MWT web-tool to facilitate clinical monitoring of neuromuscular patients

## Abstract
Neuromuscular disorders (NMDs) are rare, progressive and genetic-based conditions for which, in recent years, new personalized gene therapies are being developed. Therefore, standardized outcome measures are essential for both delineating their natural history and for describing the “modified” phenotypes. The 6MWT has become an important outcome measure for tracking the decline in walking resistance over time, particularly in dystrophinopathies. Since 2018, we have been using a modular platform, namely “Health360”, which integrates several external sources, containing some assessment tools for NMDs. Of these, the interface for the 6MWT digital tool is probably the most advanced one, allowing the clinician to monitor the overall assessment in terms of distance, events (including falls), etc. The software also allows to automatically processing the data and returns a graphical output to depict any occurring clinical change.
We report data from 32 patients (mean age: 32.8 ± 15 yrs) affected by Becker Muscular Dystrophy, with a follow-up of 1 to 4 years collected in two Italian neuromuscular centers to demonstrate the effectiveness of this tool. We also graphically show the trend of the data obtained for each patient automatically generated by the platform.
The 6MWT digital tool can be used in both clinical and research settings, making it ideal to monitor patients even in pharmacological clinical trials.
The present work was done thanks to the project InGene 2.0, funded by Regione Toscana (Bando Salute 2018)

Acta MyologicaActa MyolProposal for a model of care in a pediatric neuromuscular clinic: experience in Trento area
FioritoV IodiceA ZappaG PradalU ZuccarinoR 1
Pediatric Unit, S. Maria del Carmine Hospital in Rovereto, APSS Trento2 Child Neuropsychiatry Unit, Pediatric Department, APSS, S. Chiara Hospital, Trento3
Centro Clinico NeMO Trento, Fondazione Serena Onlus, Italy162023421 Suppl 14141–113©2023 Gaetano Conte Academy - Mediterranean Society of Myology, Naples, ItalyThis is an open access article distributed in accordance with the CC-BY-NC-ND (Creative Commons Attribution-NonCommercial-NoDerivatives 4.0 International) license. ## Abstract
Neuromuscular disorders (NMD) are a heterogeneous group of diseases with usual onset in pediatric age. Challenging needs are required and a multidisciplinary approach is the key element in the management of NMD. Subjects who live far away from the hospital referral centers, experience trouble of integrated cares due to the lack of connection between professionals, who are used to operate lonely, and often without specific expertise for NMD.
Since 2022 a multidisciplinary team with experience in the management of NMD (Pediatrician, Child neurologist, Psicologist, Physiatrist) has built up periodic evaluations with district health professionals (child neuropsychiatrist, physiotherapist) who usually take care of children with NMD. During each meeting, neurological, musculoskeletal, respiratory, cardiological, auxological and nutritional assessments were performed.
Treatments are customized for each patient and shared between the whole multidisciplinary team. This approach helps the communication with parents, increases the adherence to treatment and allows the management of local health-care professionals with specific issues to monitor.
We proposed a model of care to empower the network between referral hospital professionals and peripheral centres and to improve the quality of assistance by intercepting earlier the needs of patients with NMD and ensuring appropriate answers close to home.

Acta MyologicaActa MyolChallenging diagnosis between an atypical phenotype of motor neuron disease and myositis: a case report
BecattiniL VadiG BianchiF FontanelliL GiovanniniB MeoniC RicciG SicilianoG 1Department of Clinical and Experimental Medicine, Neurological Clinics, University of Pisa, Pisa, Italy162023421 Suppl 14141–113©2023 Gaetano Conte Academy - Mediterranean Society of Myology, Naples, ItalyThis is an open access article distributed in accordance with the CC-BY-NC-ND (Creative Commons Attribution-NonCommercial-NoDerivatives 4.0 International) license. ## Abstract
Respiratory type of Amyotrophic Lateral Sclerosis (ALS) is a rare and rapidly progressive phenotype, which can be misdiagnosed with other subacute neuromuscular diseases or paraneoplastic pictures.
Here we describe the case of an old woman showing a challenging picture of rapidly progressive hyposthenia. An 82-years-old woman came to our attention for a history of dyspnoea, head drop, and proximal upper limbs weakness started three months prior and progressively worsened. The neurologic examination showed neck and shoulder girdle hyposthenia and hypotrophy, with diffuse fasciculations. A complete paraneoplastic and infectious screening was performed, though resulting negative. CSF analysis and high camp brain MRI were normal as well, while an EMG showed a neurogenic pattern with fasciculation potentials on upper limbs, consistent with motor neuron disease. A NGS for ALS-related genes (in particular for SOD1 mutations) resulted negative. Meanwhile, myositis-related antibodies tested a few days prior, revealed a positivity for anti-Mi2 antibodies. We therefore performed muscle MRI, which detected oedema of the thighs adductor and paravertebral muscles, with signs of fat replacement. Therapeutic efforts with intravenous immunoglobulins resulted ineffective, and the patient rapidly worsened, eventually needing non-invasive mechanic ventilation. She was scheduled to undergo muscle biopsy, but she deceased the day before. She eventually showed a clinical picture more consistent with a respiratory-type of ALS.
Differential diagnosis between atypical phenotypes of ALS and inflammatory myopathies can be challenging in the acute/subacute settings, especially in cases of atypical presentation or overlapping symptoms. An accurate biological, clinical and neurophysiological assessment is mandatory to differentiate these peculiar clinical pictures.

Acta MyologicaActa MyolHigh plasma p-tau181 levels in Amyotrophic Lateral Sclerosis: clinical-electrophysiological correlations and longitudinal trajectories.
VacchianoV MastrangeloA ZenesiniC BaiardiS AvoniP PolischiB CapellariS SalviF LiguoriR ParchiP 1 Dipartimento di Scienze Biomediche e Neuromotorie, Università di Bologna, Bologna, Italy2

## Abstract
Phosphorylated-tau181 (p-tau), a specific marker of Alzheimer’s disease (AD) pathology, was unexpectedly found increased in plasma but not in cerebrospinal fluid (CSF) of patients with amyotrophic lateral sclerosis (ALS). We explored these findings in a larger ALS cohort, evaluating clinical/electrophysiological associations, prognostic value, and longitudinal trajectories of the biomarker.
We obtained baseline plasma samples from 148 ALS, 12 spinal muscular atrophy (SMA), 88 AD patients, and 60 healthy controls. Baseline CSF and longitudinal plasma samples were collected from 130 and 39 ALS patients. CSF AD markers were measured with the Lumipulse platform, and plasma p-tau with SIMOA.
ALS patients showed higher plasma p-tau levels than controls (p < 0.001) and lower than AD patients (p = 0.02). SMA patients had higher levels than controls (p = 0.03). In ALS patients, CSF p-tau and plasma p-tau did not correlate (p = 0.37), suggesting a peripheral source of the plasmatic biomarker. Plasma p-tau levels significantly increased with the number of regions showing clinical/neurophysiological LMN signs (p = 0.007) and correlated with denervation degree in the lumbosacral area (Rho = 0.51, p < 0.0001). Plasma p-tau levels were higher in classic and LMN-predominant than in bulbar phenotype (p = 0.004 and p = 0.006). Multivariate Cox regression confirmed plasma p-tau as an independent prognostic factor in ALS (HR 1.90, CI 1.25-2.90, p = 0.003). Longitudinal analysis showed a significant rise in plasma p-tau values over time, especially in fast progressors.
Plasma p-tau is increased in ALS patients, independently from CSF levels, and is firmly associated with LMN dysfunction. The finding of its putative peripheral origin deserves further investigations.

TorriF FilipponiC CiurliB LaiM AlìG VadaG PistelloM RicciG SicilianoG 1 Dipartimento di Medicina Clinica e Sperimentale, Università di Pisa, Pisa, Italia2 Centro Retrovirus, Dipartimento di Ricerca Traslazionale, Università di Pisa, Pisa, Italia3 Dipartimento di Anatomia Patologica, Università di Pisa, Pisa, Italia162023421 Suppl 14141–113©2023 Gaetano Conte Academy - Mediterranean Society of Myology, Naples, ItalyThis is an open access article distributed in accordance with the CC-BY-NC-ND (Creative Commons Attribution-NonCommercial-NoDerivatives 4.0 International) license. ## Abstract
Genetic myopathies, including muscular dystrophies, congenital and metabolic myopathies, are rare diseases that pose significant challenges both for diagnosis understanding of physiopathology and search for novel therapies. We recently started testing the use of a high-content imaging system to highlight morphologic and functional hallmarks in muscle tissue and cell cultures from patients with different myopathies in comparison with healthy controls. The system enables automated collection and analysis of images with regards to fibers number, structure and dimension, nuclei characteristics and positioning in the cell, mitochondrial activity, the autophagy machinery, proteins, metabolites, and nucleic acids. The system permits intelligent image acquisition also providing a 3D representation of the specimen and is equipped with artificial intelligence, machine-learning informed algorithms for automated quantification of volumetric and other 3D related phenotypic readouts, along with many more quantitative evaluations that the user can tailor on the specific research object. To our knowledge, this novel approach has not been widely used in the myology field. We are discussing molecular pathways involved in several pathological mechanisms to be assessed in in vitro cellular models from immortalized lines, cells cultures and tissue from patients’ biopsies. Starting with specimens from 5 control muscle biopsies and 5 pathologic biopsies of patients with muscular dystrophies and metabolic myopathies (BMD, Pompe Disease, OPMD, FSHD, LGMD, analysis of tissue and cells) we aim at providing supporting data for the use of this technique both in the diagnostic phase, physiopathology studies and drug screening for myopathies.

Acta MyologicaActa MyolExploratory study of a passive wearable device (Exoband) as walking aid in neuromuscular patients
SempliciniC AgostiniM AndrigoC NotararigoV MasieroS PiccioneF SorarùG 1UOC Neurologia Ospedale S. Antonio; Azienda Ospedale-Università di Padova2 Dipartimento di Neuroscienze, Medicina Fisica e Riabilitazione, Università degli Studi di Padova3 UOC di Neuroriabilitazione; Azienda Ospedale-Università di Padova4 Dipartimento di Neuroscienze, Università degli Studi di Padova162023421 Suppl 14141–113©2023 Gaetano Conte Academy - Mediterranean Society of Myology, Naples, ItalyThis is an open access article distributed in accordance with the CC-BY-NC-ND (Creative Commons Attribution-NonCommercial-NoDerivatives 4.0 International) license. ## Abstract
A passive wearable device (Exoband ©) was recently approved in Italy for patients with impaired walking abilities. This device is a walking brace consisting of a belt and two leg loops, connected to each other by a mechanism that stores the energy generated in the first phase of the gait cycle to return it in the second phase, thus enhancing the thrust of the hip flexors, and leading to functional walking improvement.
Aim of this exploratory study was to evaluate the effect of Exoband on motor function of neuromuscular patients with proximal weakness. We included in the study consecutive patients affected by different neuromuscular diseases (CIDP, motor polineuropathy, MND), characterized by proximal muscle involvement and gait abnormalities.
The evaluation protocol included specific walking-related outcome measures (10m walk test, Time-up-and-go, MRC), performed in standard conditions with and without the device.
Seven patients were assessed (6M, age 60-78years). A significant increase in walking velocity was observed in 10m walk test (13.4 ± 5 vs12.2 ± 4.9 sec, p < 0.05) and TUG test (13.94 ± 3.2vs 13.38 ± 2.8 sec, p < 0.05). The patients reported since the first use a subjective benefit on walking stability, speed and confidence, and less fatigue.
In conclusion, this exploratory study suggests the usefulness of a passive wearable device in neuromuscular patients with proximal weakness and gait abnormalities. Further studies are needed to evaluate the long-term effect of the regular use of Exoband, to evaluate this device in specific neuromuscular diseases, and its usefulness as rehabilitation device.

Acta MyologicaActa MyolDiaphragmatic ultrasound: a promising technique for respiratory assessment of patients with facio-scapulo-humeral muscular dystrophy (FSHD)
TorchiaE BortolaniS InchingoloR SmargiassiA MonforteM TascaG BoniniM RicheldiL RicciE 1 Università Cattolica del Sacro Cuore, Fondazione Policlinico Universitario A. Gemelli IRCCS, Roma, Italy2 Unità Operativa Complessa di Neurologia, Fondazione Policlinico Universitario A. Gemelli IRCSS, Rome, Italy162023421 Suppl 14141–113©2023 Gaetano Conte Academy - Mediterranean Society of Myology, Naples, ItalyThis is an open access article distributed in accordance with the CC-BY-NC-ND (Creative Commons Attribution-NonCommercial-NoDerivatives 4.0 International) license. ## Abstract
A restrictive respiratory impairment has been described in up to 40% of patients affected by Facio-scapulo-humeral muscular dystrophy (FSHD). Spirometry may underestimate early respiratory alterations in these patients, as inspiratory muscle impairment may occur before Forced vital capacity (FVC) variation. Ultrasonography has recently emerged as a non-invasive tool to assess the main inspiratory muscle, the diaphragm. The aim of this study was to thoroughly characterize the respiratory function of a cohort of FSHD comparing spirometric and ultrasonographic data.
Twenty-three genetically confirmed adult FSHD patients (15 male and 8 female) were enrolled. US diaphragmatic thickness at the end of a normal expiration (basal-DT), after a maximal inspiration (max-DT) and diaphragmatic excursion were calculated. The difference between max-DT and basal-DT represented “diaphragmatic thickening”. FVC, forced expiratory volume in first second (FEV1), total lung capacity (TLC) and residual volume (RV) were also assessed by spirometry. Values were compared to normative data.
US evaluation was able to detect abnormalities in 12 patients (52%), especially in diaphragmatic kinetic: median “diaphragmatic thickening” was 1.55 mm (range 0-4.6). Ten patients showed an asymmetric US pattern. A smaller portion of patients showed alterations on spirometric indexes. Inspiratory dysfunction with low TLC was detected in 6 (26%) patients, three of whom also displayed a restrictive pattern with a low FVC.
This pilot study suggests that diaphragmatic US could be a promising technique to identify early inspiratory dysfunction in FSHD patients.

For further information: https://creativecommons.org/licenses/by-nc-nd/4.0/deed.en

## Abstract
Inherited peripheral neuropathies (IPNs) are a clinically and genetically heterogeneous group of disorders affecting the peripheral nervous system. IPNs can be divided into several categories according to nerve fibres involved and nerve conduction velocity examination, with motor and sensory neuropathy (HMSN)-(CMT) being the most common form. To date more than 140 different genes related to IPNs have been identified and the number is still increasing.
We analysed a heterogeneous cohort of 83 IPN patients (56 CMT, 4 SMARD1/dSMA and 23 with generic IPN clinical indication) recruited over 2 years from 3 different centres. CMT patients were firstly screened by MLPA detecting PMP22 duplication or deletion in 50% of cases. The remaining negative CMT with the other patients of our cohort were undergone to NGS targeted sequencing using two different multigene panels (the first one including 116 INPs genes and the second one implemented with additional 52 novel genes) reaching a genetic diagnosis in 36% of patients; in 3 additional cases we found monoallelic pathogenic variants in autosomal recessive genes (SORD or PRX) and in 11 patients we detected candidate variants of until uncertain significant (VoUS). We didn’t find any notable results in 21 cases.
The most frequently mutated genes in our cohort resulted MPZ (the “hotspot” Arg98 residue was mutated in 3 patients) and IGHMBP2. In 3 cases with a single heterozygous mutation in the coding region of IGHMBP2 gene, we succeeded in finding causative mutations only sequencing the full genomic region of the gene through our second NGS custom panel.
Concluding, performing different diagnostic approaches, we reached a genetic diagnosis in almost 60% of patients, confirming that PMP22 deletion/duplication is the major genetic cause of CMT1A.

Acta MyologicaActa MyolReduction of Body Cellular Mass and Body Cellular Mass Index as an early marker of sarcopenia in Amyotrophic Lateral Sclerosis patients
GrecoG BoffaL GogliaM SeracenoS RuggieriAM MerraG RomanoL FrezzaE VietriG GruossoF NardinoG PetittaI LeurattiP De LorenzoA MassaR 1 Neuromuscular Diseases Unit, University Hospital Tor Vergata and Department of Systems Medicine, University of Rome Tor Vergata, Rome, Italy2 Section of Clinical Nutrition and Nutrigenomic, Department of Biomedicine and Prevention, University of Rome Tor Vergata, Rome, Italy162023421 Suppl 14141–113©2023 Gaetano Conte Academy - Mediterranean Society of Myology, Naples, ItalyThis is an open access article distributed in accordance with the CC-BY-NC-ND (Creative Commons Attribution-NonCommercial-NoDerivatives 4.0 International) license. ## Abstract
Amyotrophic Lateral Sclerosis (ALS) is an adult onset, neurodegenerative disease characterized by degeneration of upper and lower motor neurons. Malnutrition and sarcopenia, due to an increment of resting energy expenditure, are early manifestations of ALS and are related to poor prognosis.
Bioelectric impedance analysis (BIA) is a widespread, non-invasive, simple-to-use and cost-effective method applied in clinical practice for the assessment of body composition and nutritional status.
In this study, 27 patients of both sexes with new diagnosis of ALS and 36 unaffected controls underwent neurological examination with administration of ALS-FR and MiToS scales, collection of anthropometric measures (weight, height and BMI) and BIA.
No patient showed underweight, according to BMI. Among all the parameters provided by BIA, Body Cellular Mass (BCM) and Body Cellular Mass Index (BCMI) showed highly significant differences between ALS patients and controls, even after stratification by gender. Furthermore, comparing patients by diagnostic latency (≤ or > 12 months) or presence/absence of bulbar symptoms, no significant differences among the groups were observed. This observation indicates that these changes are independent from disease duration and presence of dysphagia.
BCM and BCMI can give information about the metabolically active cellular mass and reduction of BCMI is linked to sarcopenia. According to these results, we can infer that BCM and BCMI in particular, could be useful to determine early signs of sarcopenia in ALS patients.

## Abstract
Mitofusin-2 (MFN2) is an outer mitochondrial membrane protein essential for mitochondrial networking in most cells. Autosomal dominant mutations in the MFN2gene cause Charcot-Marie-Tooth type 2A disease (CMT2A), a severe and disabling sensory-motor neuropathy that impacts the entire nervous system. In this study, we propose a novel potential therapeutic approach combining RNA interference (RNAi) and gene therapy, whereby mutant and wild-type MFN2 mRNA are inhibited by RNA interference (RNAi), while the wild-type protein is restored by overexpressing cDNA encoding functional MFN2 modified to be resistant to RNAi.
After obtaining induced pluripotent stem cells (iPSCs) from somatic cells of CMT2A patients, we targeted the MFN2 mutant allele with specific short hairpin RNAs and simultaneously introduced a mutagenized MFN2 gene resistant to shRNA activity and encoding the native protein. We then differentiated iPSCs into spinal motor neurons (MNs) and analyzed the sub-cellular parameters previously found to be altered in CMT2A in vitro model to assess the impact of our therapy. We then evaluated this strategy in vivo in the MitoCharc1 CMT2A transgenic mouse model after cerebrospinal fluid delivery of the constructs into newborn mice using adeno-associated virus 9.
This approach significantly rescues the CMT2A MN phenotype in vitro, stabilizing the altered mitochondrial distribution and correcting abnormal mitophagic processes. This strategy also allows proper MFN2 correction in CMT2A MitoCharc1 mice.
Our results suggest that RNAi/gene therapy combined approach might represent a promising therapeutic strategy for the broad spectrum of human diseases associated with MFN2 mutations.

## Abstract
The study describes a protocol for identifying FSHD subjects by means of D4Z4 methylation assessment and Machine Learning (ML). Two independent cohorts were included in the study, namely a training group of 133 patients with clinical signs of FSHD and 150 control subjects (CTRL) and a testing set of 27 FSHD patients and 25 CTRL. The DNA methylation levels of two D4Z4 regions (DR1 and DUX4-PAS) were assessed by an in-house protocol based on bisulfite sequencing (BSS) and capillary electrophoresis with the Amplification Fragment Length Polymorphisms (AFLP) module, followed by statistical and ML analysis.
As expected, FSHD patients showed significantly reduced methylation levels compared to CTRL (FDR p < 0.001). Of note, single CpG sites were utilized to develop a ML pipeline able to discriminate FSHD subjects. The model identified four CpG sites (namely, DUX-PAS_CpG6, DUX-PAS_CpG3, DR1_CpG1, DR1_CpG22) as the most relevant for the identification of FSHD patients, reporting high metrics values (accuracy: 0.94, sensitivity: 0.93, specificity: 0.96).
Overall, the developed tool enables an accurate classification of FSHD patients, providing additional evidence for DNA methylation as a powerful biomarker for prioritizing subjects to be tested for FSHD. Moreover, the test proved to rapid (~72h) and accessible (€15/sample), further supporting its application into the clinical practice.

## Abstract
Facioscapulohumeral muscular dystrophy (FSHD) is a hereditary myopathy with a prevalence of 1:20,000. FSHD1 is associated with reduced number of D4Z4 tandem repeats located at 4q35. FSHD2 comprehends 5% of FSHD clinical cases carrying D4Z4 alleles with 11 repeat units or more. In these cases, mutations in chromatin-remodeling factors SMCHD1, DNMT3B, LRIF1 have been described. It has thus been hypothesized that FSHD results from chromatin changes determining the anomalous expression of 4q35 genes and D4Z4 reduced methylation. We designed a high-depth methylation assay testing more than 900 CpGs spanning the 3.3 kb D4Z4 repeat. Bisulfite treated DNAs from 40 subjects (26 FSHD2 index cases and 14 relatives) were tested and mapped. Bioinformatic analysis uncovered the great variability of repetitive elements. Low, intermediate and high D4Z4 methylation subdivide samples in three clusters. Of the 40 samples 16 (40%) presented reduced D4Z4 methylation at 4q and 10q. The majority, 11 samples, were from SMCHD1 mutated cases. Our analysis revealed that reduced D4Z4 methylation at 4q and 10q has no strict correlation with the clinical phenotype. Indeed, the FSHD classical phenotype (category A) was reported in 22 subjects. In 10 of them we observed reduced D4Z4 methylation. Of the 16 cases carrying SMCHD1 pathogenic variants 11 displayed the classic FSHD phenotype, 1 incomplete phenotype, 2 atypical. The new methodology we developed revealed the complexity of the epigenetic setting of repetitive elements and indicated that interpretation of D4Z4 methylation should be based on rigorous analysis and genotype-phenotype correlation.

Acta MyologicaActa MyolThe case of Friedreich’s Ataxia as an example to exploit the pleiotropic effects of nanoparticles
VillaC MistrettaM FariniA TorrenteY 1 Stem Cell Laboratory, Dino Ferrari Center, Department of Pathophysiology and Transplantation, University of Milan, Milan, Italy2 Neurology Unit, Fondazione IRCCS Ca’ Granda Ospedale Maggiore Policlinico, Milan, Italy162023421 Suppl 14141–113©2023 Gaetano Conte Academy - Mediterranean Society of Myology, Naples, ItalyThis is an open access article distributed in accordance with the CC-BY-NC-ND (Creative Commons Attribution-NonCommercial-NoDerivatives 4.0 International) license. For further information: https://creativecommons.org/licenses/by-nc-nd/4.0/deed.en

## Abstract
Neurodegenerative diseases (NDs) are a major global health burden particularly with the increasing ageing population. Hereditary predisposition and environmental risk factors contribute to the heterogeneity of existing pathological phenotypes. Traditional clinical interventions focused on the use of small drugs have often led to failures due to the difficulties of crossing the blood-brain-barrier and reaching brain. In this regard, nanosystems can specifically deliver drugs and improve their bioavailability, overcoming some of the major challenges in NDs treatment. Among NDs, Friedreich’s ataxia (FRDA) is an untreatable complex multisystemic disorder and the most spread type of ataxia, that represents a suitable target to validate the clinical potential of therapeutic strategies based on nanoparticles with pleiotropic effects. Since oxidative stress and mitochondrial dysfunctions are implicated in FRDA, we demonstrated the systemic delivery of catalysts activity of gold cluster superstructures (Au8-pXs) to improve cell response to mitochondrial reactive oxygen species (ROS) and thereby alleviate FRDA-related pathology in mesenchymal stem cells from patients and animal models with FRDA. We also found that systemic injection of Au8-pXs ameliorated motor function and cardiac contractility. These effects were associated to long-term improvement of mitochondrial functions and antioxidant cell responses. We related these events to an increased expression of frataxin, which was sustained by reduced autophagy. Overall, these results encourage further optimization of gold nanoparticles in experimental clinical strategies for the treatment of FRDA.

## Abstract
Neuromuscular diseases (NMDs) can be life-limiting (LLDs) or life-threatening diseases (LTDs) and children affected have complex needs.
We aimed to analyze the health care needs of children with NMDs enrolled in a specialistic Pediatric Palliative Care (PPC) Regional Network, using the instrument ACCAPED.
Specialistic PPC are located within an academic tertiary children’s hospital and general PPC are managed in the Regional Network. Children with NMDs are enrolled in PPC based on their health care needs since diagnosis.
We conducted a cross-sectional study of patients (0-25 years) with clinically or genetically confirmed NMD enrolled in our PPC Regional Program. ACCAPED is an Italian questionnaire that analyses 10 domains: respiration, feeding, seizures/consciousness, skin, mobility, communication, sleep, continence, drugs, and pain. The child is referred to community care if low complexity (< 29), to general PPC if middle-complexity (30–49), or to a specialized PPC service if high-complexity (350).
We collected: demographics, diagnoses, and ACCAPED scores.
At time of the study 68 patients were enrolled in our PPC Regional Network (F: 33); median age 13,2 yrs (1,6 – 25 yrs). Diagnoses: SMA 1 14/68; SMA 2 18/68; SMA 3 10/68; SMARD 2/68; dystrophy 13/68; myopathy 10/68; myasthenia 1/68. ACCAPED scores: < 29: 6/68 patients; 30 – 49: 24/68; 3 50: 38/68.
Children with NMDs have complex needs. Although some children have low clinical complexity scores, they have high social demands. PPC embraces physical, emotional, and unmet social needs and is the right answer for children with NMDs and their families.

PacilioS CramerT CostaR PapaV RodiaMT FraboniB FocareteML CenacchiG 1 Department of Biomedical and Neuromotor Sciences DIBINEM, Alma Mater Studiorum- University of Bologna, Italy2 Applied Biomedical Research Center–CRBA, IRCCS St. Orsola Hospital, Alma Mater Studiorum University of Bologna, Italy3 Department of Chemistry “Giacomo Ciamician”, Alma Mater Studiorum University of Bologna, Italy4 Department of Physics and Astronomy “Augusto Righi”, Alma Mater Studiorum University of Bologna, Italy162023421 Suppl 14141–113©2023 Gaetano Conte Academy - Mediterranean Society of Myology, Naples, ItalyThis is an open access article distributed in accordance with the CC-BY-NC-ND (Creative Commons Attribution-NonCommercial-NoDerivatives 4.0 International) license. ## Abstract
Several diseases are caused by skeletal muscle (SKM) dysfunction, and existing therapies, while offering some relief, are only partially effective. In order to develop new therapies, there is a high demand for technologies capable of examining SKM function. In light of this, tissue engineering is a promising tool to create 3D tissue-like structures by combining living cells, biocompatible materials and appropriate biochemical and biophysical elements. We examined the role of an electrospun scaffold made of biodegradable and biocompatible polymers in SKM myogenesis to study SKM functionalities. The 3D porous nanofibrous scaffold was obtained by electrospinning poly(L-lactide-co-ε-caprolactone) (PLCL) and it was coated with collagen before cell culture experiments. Myoblasts were seeded on the scaffold, and the cell morphology and differentiation pathway was analyzed. In the in vitro assay the mechanical response of the engineered tissue was measured with Atomic Force Microscopy. Electrical stimulation of muscle cells was achieved by driving a bipolar current stimulus through the porous scaffold. Above a current threshold, myoblast contraction and related tissue movement was observed. The approach offers a well-controlled and quantitative analysis of SKM conditions, representing a valid instrument for studying tissue functionality in vitro. We developed a screening model that can offer new insights into SKM function and dysfunction. This model can help the study of SKM pathophysiology without the need for in vivo animal testing and can provide an alternative to drug testing.

BonannoS DevigiliG TogniR CorradiM LanteriP MantegazzaR EleopraR MaggiL 1 Neurology IV, Neuroimmunology and Neuromuscular Diseases Unit, Fondazione IRCCS Istituto Neurologico Carlo Besta, Milan, Italy2 Parkinson and Movement Disorders Unit, Fondazione IRCCS Istituto Neurologico Carlo Besta, Milan, Italy3 Neurophysiopathology Unit, Fondazione IRCCS Istituto Neurologico Carlo Besta, Milan, Italy162023421 Suppl 14141–113©2023 Gaetano Conte Academy - Mediterranean Society of Myology, Naples, ItalyThis is an open access article distributed in accordance with the CC-BY-NC-ND (Creative Commons Attribution-NonCommercial-NoDerivatives 4.0 International) license. ## Abstract
There is a need for outcome measures to assess disease progression and treatments effect in adult spinal muscular atrophy (SMA). High-resolution ultrasonography (US) is an established tool in neuromuscular disorders but only preliminary reports in small SMA populations have been currently produced.
Here, we aimed to investigate a wide range of peripheral nerves in adult SMA patients using US to detail in vivo nerve morphology, in view of the potential role of US as imaging biomarker.
Thirteen adult SMA patients (1 SMA type 2, 12 SMA type 3; 6 females; 5 sitters and 8 walkers; disease duration 34.7 ± 15.5y) were compared with 25 healthy subjects, 14 ALS patients and 26 patients with sensory-motor diabetic polyneuropathy. We performed US studies of 9 among motor and sensory nerves, the brachial plexus and three cervical roots.
The nerve cross-sectional area (CSA) of the C5, C6 and C7 nerve roots and of the vagus nerve were smaller in SMA versus the other groups. Only in SMA patients the upper arm nerve trunks were significantly thinner compared with the forearm, with conserved fascicle density (FD). The FD was significantly related to the muscular-US echogenicity at Heckmatt scales and to the MRC-strength scale (p < 0.0001); both CSA and FD were lower in sitter versus walkers (p < 0.0001). CSAs in the lower limbs were thinner than healthy controls but did not differ with the other patients (p 0.55).
Nerve-US was able to identify microstructural features specific to SMA patients and could be considered a candidate tool for longitudinal studies.

## Abstract
Health360 is a modular software platform, integrating external sources to guarantee an effective, agile data collection from patients with neuromuscular diseases. The modular structure of the tool makes possible to purposely develop a range of modules with own databases and different kinds of data, making Health360 potentially adaptable to other clinical conditions or use case scenarios, therefore representing the ideal solution for data collection in research projects and, eventually, within small clinical centers.
Among the modules already released by the developers is the “Trials”. The aim of this module is to manage clinical trials focused on a set of data and events that can be derived from the clinical history of the patients, aimed at studying similarities and trends. The module is completely customizable based on the Case Report Form (CRF). Data and fields within the CRF are organized into “groups” based on their features and on the back-ends they are stored into. Each group is associated with a plug-in within the Trials module. In every plug-in, the information retrieved from the back-ends are rendered and filtered based on the CRF fields indicated within the configuration file. Then, the information are composed within the Trials module as a spreadsheet layout (patients’ data, examinations, anamnesis, laboratory tests, etc.). From each of the viewed fields, it is possible to access to the patients’ sheet to modify data or to view other relevant information.
Finally, the enrollment criteria of the patients within the Health360 platform are totally customizable, therefore adaptable to specific trials.
The present work was done thanks to the project InGene 2.0, funded by Regione Toscana (Bando Salute 2018).

## Abstract
The interpretation of titin gene (TTN) variants often requires complex analyses, including deep phenotyping, messenger RNA and protein studies.
Aiming to address the interpretation of TTN variants in muscle progenitors and providing insight into pathogenesis, we isolated myoblasts from 2 infants affected by centronuclear myopathy carrying nonsense biallelic variants in different exons of TTN.
We performed mRNA studies and Western Blot (WB) to understand the molecular consequences of the variants. mRNA analysis was able to highlight the splicing defects in canonical and non-canonical splice sites. At WB, depending of antibody used, patients showed reduce or absent full length titin, and different pattern of titin smaller isoforms bands.
With confocal high-content imaging associated to cell painting techniques, we performed an in-depth cellular characterization of myoblasts. We first noted that myoblasts at 21 days presented the same proliferation rate as controls.
Then, to explore possible defect in sarcomer organization, we analysed expression and distribution of titin and several other sarcomeric proteins in both cell lines and muscle sections.
In muscle sections, the pattern of titin expression resulted altered albeit present in both patients. Among titin partners, the thin filaments proteins appeared reduced or irregular, compared to controls. Of note, the pattern was dissimilar among patients with different mutations. In cells lines, similarly to sections, titin and other proteins of the sarcomere appeared differently expressed compared to controls, however the differences are not statistically significant.
We conclude that even nonsense mutations of titin do not significantly affect myoblasts survival and have minor impact on sarcomere organization in muscle progenitors.

Acta MyologicaActa MyolZebrafish models as new tools to study Sarcoglycanopathies
Dalla BarbaF CarottiM BenetolloA ScanoM CaccinP ArgentonF SandonàD 1Department of Biomedical Sciences1 Department of Biology, University of Padova, Padova, Italy162023421 Suppl 14141–113©2023 Gaetano Conte Academy - Mediterranean Society of Myology, Naples, ItalyThis is an open access article distributed in accordance with the CC-BY-NC-ND (Creative Commons Attribution-NonCommercial-NoDerivatives 4.0 International) license. ## Abstract
Sarcoglycanopathies are severe autosomal recessive limb-girdle muscular dystrophies caused by mutations in alpha, beta, gamma and delta-sarcoglycan (SG) coding genes. Sarcoglycanopathies are characterized by the absence/strong reduction of the SG-complex, which is essential in protecting sarcolemma from muscle contraction stress. The clinical phenotype is characterized by progressive muscle weakness with variable respiratory and cardiac involvement.
For in vivo modelling of sarcoglycanopathies, valuable for phenotype-based screening of new drugs, we have generated and characterized novel zebrafish lines in which either beta- or delta-SG were targeted exploiting the CRISPR/Cas9 technology. The single SG-KO lines present a mild phenotype at the larval stage. Therefore, to accelerate the onset of disease traits, we used two approaches: - delta-SG-KO zebrafish, immediately after hatching, were placed in a viscous medium to challenge muscle in stressful conditions; - a double knock out zebrafish (bd-SG-DKO) was obtained by breeding the single SG-KOs.
In both cases, clear and quantifiable dystrophic traits were present since the earliest stages of the development. This allows planning in vivo screening campaigns of small molecules.
As most of the reported sarcoglycanopathy cases are due to missense mutations, we generated the E264K-delta-SG-KI. This zebrafish line expresses a low level of the delta-SG protein, suggesting that the mutant is recognized as folding defective and prematurely degrades as in humans; as expected the myopathic features appear under stressful conditions only. This novel model carrying a SG missense mutant is of particular interest for testing compounds helping the folding process of defective SG proteins.

RonfiniM Di MauroV PrandoV Franco-RomeroA MazzaroA FavaroG Lo VersoF DokshokovaL ArmaniA SorarùG GuesciniM CatalucciD MongilloM SandriM ZagliaT 1 Department of Biomedical Sciences, University of Padova, Padova, Italy2

162023421 Suppl 14141–113©2023 Gaetano Conte Academy - Mediterranean Society of Myology, Naples, ItalyThis is an open access article distributed in accordance with the CC-BY-NC-ND (Creative Commons Attribution-NonCommercial-NoDerivatives 4.0 International) license. ## Abstract
Muscle-restricted block of autophagy, as occurring in MLC1f-Atg7-/- mice, is accompanied to increased release of extracellular vesicles (ECVs), enriched in the muscle-specific microRNA, miR-206, which was found elevated in the plasma and heart of MLC-Atg7-/- mice. Since miR-206 it not expressed in the heart, in physiologic conditions, we assessed its effect on cardiac homeostasis.
Standard histology in heart sections from MLC-Atg7-/- mice did not reveal morphological alterations. However, IF staining and 3-D reconstruction of the neuronal network in heart blocks showed SN fragmentation and reduced SN density. Consistent with SN denervation, heart rate increase, upon atropine administration, was reduced in MLC-Atg7-/- mice, which also showed arrhythmias. Similar results were obtained in normal mice injected with ECVs carrying miR-206, supporting the primary role of miR-206 in affecting heart neurons. Consistently, ECV-carried miR-206 directly targets cultured cardiac SNs, leading to neurodegeneration. Bioinformatics, luciferase and molecular/biochemical assays indicate that the neuronal effects are due to miR-206 downregulating the low-affinity NGF receptor, p75. This impairs formation of p75/TrkA heterodimers, which alters pro-survival NGF transport, resulting in SN degeneration. Notably, these results were confirmed in vivo.
Our results indicate that a primary muscle defect leads to release, into the bloodstream, of miR-206 which may affect long-distance tissues, i.e. the myocardium. In addition, we uncover a novel target of miR-206, affecting cardiac innervation. These results may be accountable for cardiac dysfunction secondary to skeletal muscle defects, a common feature of neurodegenerative disorders characterized by increased miR-206 plasma levels (i.e. DMD, ALS).

## Abstract
Recessive and dominant variants in POLRMT gene, encoding the mitochondrial RNA polymerase involved in the transcription of the mitochondrial genome, have been recently described in patients showing variable presentations including global developmental delay, hypotonia, short stature, muscle weakness and speech/intellectual disability in childhood.
A 17 years-old boy came to our attention presenting childhood onset of progressive spastic paraparesis. Neurological examination showed increased tone at lower limbs and bright osteotendinous reflexes with paraparetic gait. Babinski sign was present bilaterally. Strength and tone at upper limbs, sensation as far as coordination were normal. At cranial nerves, spontaneous nystagmus at right gaze was present. Developmental delay and intellectual disability were absent. Brain MRI showed signs of leukoencephalopathy with bilateral, symmetric and diffuse T2/FLAIR hyperintensity of sovra-ventricular white matter, and hyperintensity of piramidal tracts. Spine MRI showed hypotrophy of the dorsal tract. Clinical exome analysis ruled out HSP-related genes.
Electromyography showed myopathic signs while creatine kinase levels were normal.
Whole exome sequencing detected two POLRMT variants: the nonsense substitution c.2775C > A (p.Cys925*) previously reported as pathogenetic, and the rare missense change c.3326A > G (p.Lys1109Arg). Both mutations were present in patient’s sister, who shared the same clinical phenotype.
A muscle biopsy was performed without disclosing specific changes. Respiratory chain complex activity analysis on muscle biopsy showed mild decrease of cI+III (30%) and cIV (20%) complexes. Muscle MRI showed mild inflammatory signs at soleus and glutei.
This report further enlarges the clinical spectrum of POLRMT related disorders.

Acta MyologicaActa MyolWhole miRNome profiling in Myasthenia Gravis thymus: novel insights into the molecular events underlying autoimmunity
TarascoMC IacominoN ScandiffioL BortoneF MarcuzzoS BonannoS Rita FrangiamoreR PistilloD VoulazE AlloisioM MaggiL AntozziC MantegazzaR CavalcanteP 1 Neurology 4, Neuroimmunology and Neuromuscular Diseases Unit, Fondazione IRCCS Istituto Neurologico Carlo Besta, Milan, Italy2 Ph.D. Program in Neuroscience, University of Milano-Bicocca, Monza, Italy3 Center for Biological Resources, Humanitas Cancer Center, IRCCS Humanitas Research Hospital, Rozzano, Milan, Italy4 Department of Biomedical Sciences, Humanitas University, Milan, Italy5 Thoracic Surgery Division, IRCCS Humanitas Research Hospital, Rozzano, Milan, Italy162023421 Suppl 14141–113©2023 Gaetano Conte Academy - Mediterranean Society of Myology, Naples, ItalyThis is an open access article distributed in accordance with the CC-BY-NC-ND (Creative Commons Attribution-NonCommercial-NoDerivatives 4.0 International) license. ## Abstract
Myasthenia gravis (MG) is a prototypic autoimmune disorder affecting neuromuscular junction, mainly caused by anti-acetylcholine receptor (AChR) antibodies that lead to muscle weakness and fatigability. The thymus is the main site of autoimmunity development in AChR-MG patients, who mostly present thymic abnormalities, including follicular hyperplasia (MG-FH) and thymoma (MG-T). MicroRNAs (miRNAs) are key regulators of innate and adaptive immune response, but their role in the intra-thymic MG pathogenesis is not completely known. We performed whole miRNome sequencing in MG thymuses (MG-FH, n = 8; MG-T, n = 8), normal thymuses (CTR, n = 8) and thymomas from non-MG patients (T, n = 8) followed by validation of differentially expressed (DE) miRNAs, and analysis of predicted target genes in the same and additional (MG-FH, n = 15; MG-T, n = 16; NT, n = 7; T, n = 19) tissues by qPCR. MiRNA sequencing identified 27 up- and 18 down-regulated miRNAs in MG-FH compared to CTR, and 102 up- and 61 down-regulated miRNAs in MG-T compared to T. Gene Ontology terms associated with DE miRNAs were enriched in TGF-β signaling-related processes. Validation and miRNA/target gene correlation analyses revealed dysregulation of miR-150-5p/STAT1, miR-486-5p/PTEN, and miR-20b-5p/PTEN pairs in MG-FH, and of the miR-20b-5p/PTEN/SMAD2/STAT3/RORγ molecular axis in MG-T. Due to the well-known role of STAT1, PTEN, CREB1, and SMAD2 in TGF-β pathways modulating regulatory T/Th17 cell balance, we suggested a contribution of the identified miRNAs to immune dysregulation in MG thymus. Our overall findings set the basis for future development of miRNA-based immunomodulatory treatments for improving MG care.
Work supported by the Italian Ministry of Health (RRC, GR-2013-02358564, RF-2016-02364384).

Acta MyologicaActa MyolMicroRNAs as biomarkers of responsiveness to immunosuppressive drugs in Myasthenia Gravis: towards personalized medicine
IacominoN TarascoMC BallardiniM FrangiamoreR VanoliF MaggiL AntozziC FotiM MantegazzaR CavalcanteP 1 Neurology 4, Neuroimmunology and Neuromuscular Diseases Unit, Fondazione IRCCS Istituto Neurologico Carlo Besta, Milan, Italy2 Ph.D. program in Neuroscience, University of Milano-Bicocca, Monza, Italy3 Department of Medicine and Surgery, University of Milano-Bicocca, Monza, Italy162023421 Suppl 14141–113©2023 Gaetano Conte Academy - Mediterranean Society of Myology, Naples, ItalyThis is an open access article distributed in accordance with the CC-BY-NC-ND (Creative Commons Attribution-NonCommercial-NoDerivatives 4.0 International) license. ## Abstract
Myasthenia Gravis (MG) is a chronic disabling autoimmune disease affecting neuromuscular junction. Adoption of international consensus guidelines for MG treatment proved to be clinically effective, but most patients fail to achieve complete stable remission, requiring lifelong immunosuppressive (IS) therapy, and a proportion of them (~10-15%) is refractory to IS drugs. Inter-individual variation in drug efficacy highlights the need to develop personalized medicine (PM) approaches, to improve therapeutic success and cost-effectiveness ratio of treatments. MicroRNAs (miRNAs) are promising PM biomarkers for immune-mediated diseases, since they are able to modulate both immune response and drug metabolism. We performed whole miRNome profiling in peripheral blood cells of MG patients stratified as responder (R) and non-responder (NR) to conventional IS drugs using microarrays, and then validated differentially expressed miRNAs in 41 patients (21 R and 20 NR) by qPCR. We revealed a significant decrease of miR-150-5p, key “immune-miR” regulating T and B cell functions, in NR compared to R patients, and obtained sensitivity and specificity results indicative of the ability of this miRNA to discriminate between the two patients’ groups. We also confirmed our previous data showing an association between the “miR-485-3p, -409-3p, -323b-3p (14q32.31)” cluster and non-responsiveness to IS therapies in MG patients. Our overall findings disclose the role of specific miRNAs as biomarkers to monitor IS drug efficacy in MG, potentially useful for early identification of NR patients to be directed towards other therapeutic options, such as biological drugs.
Work supported by the Italian Ministry of Health (RRC, RF-2016-02364384).

## Abstract
Myofibrillar myopathies (MFMs) are part of the Protein aggregate myopathies (PAMs), a group of muscle disorders characterized by protein aggregates, autophagic vacuoles and disintegration of myofibrils in muscle fibers. When protein aggregates cannot be removed through the narrow pore of the proteasome, they accumulate into larger deposits, called aggresomes and are eliminate through aggrephagy, a specialized form of macroautophagy. Different proteins have been identified as key players of this pathway, namely p62/SQSTM1, NBR1, Alfy/WDFY3, HDAC6, Tollip, TAX1BP1, OPTN and CCT2.
In this study we aimed to investigate the role of aggrephagy in a cohort of 29 MFM patients, particularly by evaluating the expression of p62 and NBR1 as well as FK2 a marker of ubiquitinated proteins. Specifically, we analyzed 8 DES, 6 DNAJB6, 2 FLNC, 7 MYOT and 6 TTN mutated patients’ with mean age at muscle biopsy 50 ± 29 years. Through immunofluorescence, we investigated the correlation between aggrephagy biomarkers and molecular and clinical features, in particular disease severity at time of muscle biopsy and at the end of the follow-up period. Using the binomial distribution as probability model, preliminary data showed that NBR1 is mostly increased in MYOT mutated patients, in patients with high score for the Walton-Gardner and Medwin scale at last visit and in patients with earlier age of onset.
Preliminary data suggested aggrephagy activation in MFM and possible correlation between aggrephagy biomarkers and specific clinical and molecular data.

## Abstract
In muscle cells subjected to mechanical stimulation, LINC complex and cytoskeletal proteins are basic to reorganize nucleus-cytoskeleton architecture and to maintain nuclei orientation. In this context, the lamin A/C, a LINC-complex protein, plays a role in the remodeling of nuclear protein scaffold, with a mechanism that remains mostly elusive. This study demonstrates that in human myoblasts subjected to mechanical stretching, lamin A/C is able to recruit desmin and plectin to the nuclear envelope, allowing a proper spatial orientation of the nuclei. Interestingly, in human myoblasts carrying EDMD2-causative LMNA mutations, exposed to mechanical stimulation, the recruitment of desmin and plectin to the nucleus and nuclear orientation were impaired, suggesting that a functional lamin A/C is crucial for the response to muscular strain. By describing a new mechanism of action headed by lamin A/C in the nuclear platform remodeling during mechanical stress, these findings show a structural alteration that could be involved in the onset of the muscular defects observed in these laminopathies.

Acta MyologicaActa MyolDifferential dysferlin expression in human and rat fast and slow skeletal muscle suggests different role
MeznaricM AngeliniC 1
University of Ljubljana, Faculty of Medicine, Ljubljana, Slovenia2
University of Padova, Department of Neurosciences, Padova, Italy162023421 Suppl 14141–113©2023 Gaetano Conte Academy - Mediterranean Society of Myology, Naples, ItalyThis is an open access article distributed in accordance with the CC-BY-NC-ND (Creative Commons Attribution-NonCommercial-NoDerivatives 4.0 International) license. ## Abstract
A common form of limb girdle muscular dystrophy (LGMD) is dysferlinopathy presenting as LGMDR2/2B or distal Miyoshi myopathy, that show different clinical course. They are caused by mutations in the DYSF gene encoding the protein dysferlin, a transmembrane sarcolemmal protein which has a role in the repair of sarcolemma. Dysferlin is also a T-tubule protein.
To the best of our knowledge dysferlin expression in different skeletal muscles has not been studied so far. We investigated by western blot dysferlin – myosin ratio in human skeletal muscles, biceps brachii and tibialis anterior and rat muscles, the slow soleus and fast extensor digitorum longus (EDL). The ratio was higher in human neuromuscular controls in biceps brachii than in tibialis anterior. In six rat muscles the ratio was significantly higher in soleus than in EDL. Our results show in human a trend towards a higher “content” of dysferlin in fast skeletal muscle compared to slow which might be relevant for the different course observed in LGMDR2/2B and Miyoshi myopathy. The differential expression of dysferlin in rat muscle might be related to metabolic role besides the repair mechanisms. In conclusion, in four neuromuscular controls with no weakness and in six rat muscles our western blot results have discovered a different ratio of dysferlin – myosin in slow and fast muscles. This implies a metabolic role of dysferlin, besides the repair, on type 1 fibres that are rich in mitochondria and lipids and in type 2 fibres with abundant T-tubules.

## Abstract
Transportin-3, encoded by the TNPO3 gene, is physiologically involved in the translocation to the nucleus of many different proteins, such as SR proteins, which are associated with RNA metabolism. Mutations in the termination codon of the TNPO3 gene have been proved to cause Limb Girdle Muscular Dystrophy D2 (LGMD D2). More specifically, a single nucleotide deletion leads to the production of a protein with a 15 amino acid extension of its C-terminal domain. This project aimed to investigate the cause of LGMD D2, utilizing an in vivo approach, focusing on the role of TNPO3 on muscle development and to identify any potential molecular pathways linked to the disorder. This procedure involved the microinjection of mRNAs encoding the mutated form of human TNPO3 into Zebrafish embryos, to follow its effects on the myogenic processes during development, considering that primary myogenesis is completed by 48 hours post-fertilization. The subsequent analysis revealed changes in the gene expression profiles of Myogenic Regulatory Factors (MRFs) and muscle-specific proteins. The transcriptomic alterations are reflected on a phenotypical level by an aberrant organization of muscle fibres, as demonstrated by immunofluorescence staining. The methods we employed enabled us to gain an initial understanding of the role of Transportin-3 in muscle development and the pathogenic mechanism of its mutated form underlying LGMD D2, which has yet to be determined.

BonannoS MalacarneC MattioliE SaracenoF CavalcanteP Santovito SpiroL CheliM FiorilloC AndreettaF MantegazzaR MarcuzzoS MaggiL LattanziG 1 Neurology IV, Neuroimmunology and Neuromuscular Diseases Unit, Fondazione IRCCS Istituto Neurologico Carlo Besta2

## Abstract
Emery-Dreifuss muscular dystrophy (EDMD) is mainly inherited in an X-linked (EDMD1) and autosomal dominant (EDMD2) manner, ranging from early onset severe-childhood to slowly progressive adulthood phenotypes. Common features are joint contractures, humero-peroneal muscle weakness with later scapular-pelvic girdle muscles involvement, and cardiopathy. The pathogenic and clinical variability makes the correct diagnostic framing often not initially clear hence, combining clinical evaluation with serum biomarkers would enable conclusive early diagnosis.
Cardiac and skeletal muscles are enriched in a group of specific microRNAs (miRNAs), called myomiRs (miR-1, miR-133a, miR-133b, miR-206) which have a crucial role in myogenic processes and muscle homeostasis as they target several muscle-related genes that trigger and sustain muscle atrophy.
Here, we investigated for the first time myomiRs, and miR-21, a critical promoter of fibrosis, involvement and their potential clinical use in EDMDs.
We analysed myomiR and miR-21 expression levels in EDMD1-patient derived myoblasts and tenocytes, and in the respective CRISPR-corrected cells. An up-regulation of miR-206, miR-133a, miR-133b and miR-1 specifically in myoblasts after efficient correction of the genotype, and the down-regulation of miR-21 both in corrected tenocytes and myoblasts was observed. Further, we examined the expression levels of the myomiRs and miR-21 in serum of 21 adult EDMD2 patients, and in age-matched healthy controls. We demonstrated a significant up-regulation of miR-206 and miR-21 in serum of EDMD2 patients compared to controls (p < 0.0001).
Our findings point out myomiRs and miR-21 as potential diagnostic markers and therapeutic targets for EDMDs.

## Abstract
The gut microbiota is central player in shaping and modulating immune system responses, with gut microbial dysbiosis linked to several autoimmune and immune-mediated diseases. In patients affected by Duchenne Muscular Dystrophy (DMD), the lack of dystrophin and dystrophin glycoprotein complex proteins is likely to cause dysfunctions in the structural integrity of the intestinal barrier. Altered bowel permeability can represent a source of molecules that might produce muscle wasting by entering the circulation, via a disrupted gut barrier, and travelling toward muscles. In addition, this condition could be responsible for the uncontrolled passage of components of the microbiota towards the immune cells of the lamina propria, where they induce the pathological activation of these cells. Here, we demonstrate dysbiosis in mouse model of DMD (mdx) that is associated with alterations of peripheral and muscle immune landscape. Treatment with broad-spectrum antibiotics depleting the gut microbiota in mdx mice determined reduced muscle inflammation and enrichment of catabolic and autophagic processes associated with muscle wasting. We also show that Germ-free-mdx mice were impaired in muscle function except for reduced fibrosis and absence of chronic muscle inflammation. Furthermore, intestinal colonization of mdx mice with eubiotic microbiota was sufficient to reduce inflammation improving muscle pathology and function. From a therapeutic perspective, our results allow the identification of intestinal microorganisms, microbial products, and metabolites as potential targets to tailor innovative therapeutic strategies for the treatment of DMD patients.

D’AgostinoS MagarottoF TafuroG GermanoG BizC SemenzatoA GambaP MuracaM PozzobonM 1 Women’s and Children’s Health Department, University of Padova, Padova, Italy2
Institute of Pediatric Research Città della Speranza, Padova, Italy3 Department of Pharmaceutical and Pharmacological Sciences, University of Padova, Padova, Italy4 Department of Surgery, Oncology and Gastroenterology DiSCOG, Orthopedic Clinic, University of Padova, Padova, Italy162023421 Suppl 14141–113©2023 Gaetano Conte Academy - Mediterranean Society of Myology, Naples, ItalyThis is an open access article distributed in accordance with the CC-BY-NC-ND (Creative Commons Attribution-NonCommercial-NoDerivatives 4.0 International) license. ## Abstract
The employment of decellularized muscle as scaffold for muscle reconstruction has been recognized as a successful strategy for regenerative medicine. Indeed biochemical and mechanical stimuli driving cell growth and differentiation, are maintained after the decellularization process. However, the method chosen to produce the scaffold deeply influences its properties and the recellularization efficiency. Therefore, the aim of this work was to study the best method to decellularize human muscle and to prove the recellularization efficiency.
Human muscle biopsies underwent decellularization with two detergents (sodium deoxycholate and sodium dodecyl sulphate) and latrunculin B. Samples were analyzed for residual DNA content, ECM component preservation (collagen network, laminin, glycosaminoglycans), and mechanical properties. Finally, in the matrices produced, human muscle precursor cells were injected to evaluate the recellularization efficiency.
The usage of detergents allowed the obtainment of an 85% reduction in DNA content, but muscle ECM architecture and composition was worsened with detergents in respect with Latruculin B. In addition, a higher amount of residual sulphated glycosaminoglycans was found after treatment with latrunculin B, while the detergents clearly compromised the glycocalyx. Mechanical properties were tested and seeding efficiency was higher with Latruculin B, considering cell distribution, proliferation and myogenic features.
Latrunculin B, already used in clinic for different purposes, can represent a new strategy to produce biocompatible human muscle scaffold useful for tissue engineering purposes.

## Abstract
LGMDR3, LGMDR4, LGMDR5 are recessive autosomal diseases, there are no specific treatments.

Acta MyologicaActa MyolA potential treatment for sarcoglycanopathy: pharmacological profile of C17 CFTR corrector and its efficacy with a new regimen of administration
BenetolloA ScanoM ParrasiaS BiasuttoL Dalla BarbaF CarottiM NogaraL BlaauwB SandonàD 1 Department of Biomedical Sciences,2 Department of Biology, University of Padova, Padova, Italy3 National Research Council, Neuroscience Institute, Padova, Italy162023421 Suppl 14141–113©2023 Gaetano Conte Academy - Mediterranean Society of Myology, Naples, ItalyThis is an open access article distributed in accordance with the CC-BY-NC-ND (Creative Commons Attribution-NonCommercial-NoDerivatives 4.0 International) license. ## Abstract
Sarcoglycanopathies (LGMDR3-6) are rare autosomal recessive diseases due to mutation in sarcoglycan (SG) genes, characterized by the progressive weakness of skeletal muscles of shoulder and pelvic girdle. In sarcoglycanopathies, most genetic defects (65%) are missense mutations leading to folding-defective proteins, which, even though potentially functional, are removed by the cell’s quality-control system. Treatments with the small molecule C17, a CFTR corrector, successfully rescued the human mutant R98H-α-SG in a mouse model of LGMDR3, resulting in phenotype and functional recovery. This compound helps the mutant protein to skip from degradation and to reach the sarcolemma resulting in the assembly of the SG-complex. Thus, we started investigating the pharmacological properties of this very promising compound by performing ADME (adsorption, distribution, metabolism and elimination) studies. Data obtained from these analyses showed that the compound is quickly absorbed from the site of injection, well distributed in the organism, metabolized at the level of the small intestine and eliminated through faeces (both unmodified and modified) and urine (modified). The presence in skeletal muscle of a residual amount of the compound after 48 hours from the parenteral administration, moved us to design of a regimen of administration in which the compound was injected every 48 hours. The treatment outcome was positive, resulting in the effective recovery of the tibialis anterior muscle force as a consequence of the rescue at the sarcolemma of the SG-complex, even though containing a mutated subunit.

CoxD HendersonM ParkhurstY GaitondeP DasturR EmmonsSS BarresiR 1 John Walton Muscular Dystrophy Research Centre, Faculty of Medical Sciences, Translational & Clinical Research Institute, Newcastle University, Newcastle upon Tyne, UK2 Muscle Immunoanalysis Unit, Newcastle upon Tyne Hospitals NHS Foundation Trust, NHS England Highly Specialised Service for Rare Neuromuscular Disorders (LGMD), Newcastle upon Tyne, UK3

IRCCS San Camillo Hospital, Venice, Italy162023421 Suppl 14141–113©2023 Gaetano Conte Academy - Mediterranean Society of Myology, Naples, ItalyThis is an open access article distributed in accordance with the CC-BY-NC-ND (Creative Commons Attribution-NonCommercial-NoDerivatives 4.0 International) license. ## Abstract
Mutations in the DYSF gene cause dysferlinopathy, a rare autosomal recessive muscular dystrophy with either proximal or distal onset. Absence of dysferlin expression assessed by immunoblot of muscle biopsies or CD14+ blood monocytes is a specific diagnostic indicator of the disease. We have developed a simplified immunohistochemical assay to detect dysferlin expression in neutrophils on peripheral blood films (PBF). In the present study, we validated the assay on a cohort of individuals of Indian background with genetic diagnosis of dysferlinopathy. 32 patients and 18 healthy individuals attending the Dysferlin Registry meeting “Strength in Numbers” in Mumbai (coordinated by Jain Foundation in November 2019) volunteered for the study. PBF immunolabeled for dysferlin were blind-scored and results were correlated with DYSF genetic status of participants. Labelling for dysferlin was absent or very reduced in all patients. Negative labelling was also seen in control PBF that were of poor quality or stored at room temperature higher than average for a prolonged time. In contrast, storage at 4°C for up to 16 weeks did not affect the specificity of the test. The results of this study suggest great promise for the neutrophil assay as an inexpensive and rapid screening for the presence of DYSF mutations. Further studies are planned, which include participants in whom the disease status is unknown, to investigate how the assay is likely to perform in clinical practice as a pre-screening for mutations in the DYSF gene and as a functional assessment for variants’ pathogenicity.

## Abstract
Variants in the CAPN3 gene cause the most frequent autosomal recessive limb-girdle muscular dystrophy: LGMDR1. During the last years, different variants in CAPN3 have been described in families presenting with an autosomal dominant (AD) myopathy, characterized by a milder phenotype and later onset (LGMDD4), thus challenging the exclusively recessive inheritance of this disorder.
We report two siblings, referred to the Neuromuscular outpatient clinic of the University of Genova for persistent high serum levels of CPK (> 1000 U/L), associated to mild, but progressive limb girdle weakness, particularly in the eldest, with difficulty climbing stairs. Moreover, the father of our patients had also progressive proximal weakness, suggesting an AD inheritance of the myopathy.
A muscular biopsy on the most symptomatic brother revealed signs of muscle impairment (scattered hypotrophic fibers, nuclear centralization, and fiber splitting).
Next-generation sequencing of genes causative of hyperCKemia and LGMD revealed a heterozygous CAPN3 missense variant c.1478G > A (p.Arg493Gln) in both siblings. This variant affects a highly conserved residue and is therefore predicted to be clinically relevant. MLPA analysis excluded copy number variations in CAPN3 gene and whole-exome sequencing was negative for mutations in other genes that could justify the clinical picture. Western blot analysis of calpain-3 protein was normal, as already described for another CAPN3 variant, close to the one identified in our family and also associated to LGMDD4.
We reported the first description of the p.Arg493Gln variant in CAPN3 as a possible cause of mild calpainopathy with AD inheritance. The pathogenicity of this isolated variant needs additional studies.

PolitoG C CipriettiC MelchiorreS AdjnajP Di MuzioA 1Clinica Neurologica, Ospedale S.S. Annunziata, Chieti, Italy✉Corresponding author.

## Abstract
Facio-scapular-humeral dystrophy (FSHD) is the third most common muscular disorder. It is a genetically determined disease that causes progressive weakness, usually asymmetrical of the musculature of the face, scapula, upper limbs and pelvic girdle.
In this study, we conducted an epidemiological analysis of the patient cohort from Abruzzo Regional Centre for Neuromuscular Diseases. From this court of patients, we then identified their group characteristics, genetics, clinical and rating scales to investigate the degree of dystrophy. Starting from the medical history and neurological examination, 167 (M:F = 46:53, Mean age 50.8 +- 19.6) patients performed genetic analysis for FSHD, 99 had deletion at the D4Z4 locus compatible with FSHD. 64 patients with compatible genetics and clinical criteria were diagnosed with FSHD. Of the 64 patients with a genetic and clinical diagnosis 55 were from Abruzzo. In this patient subset the overall penetrance D4Z4 deletion was 72.4 %.
The epidemiological analysis performed confirms that FSHD is one of the most frequent neuromuscular dystrophies in the Italian population and specifically in Abruzzo.

Acta MyologicaActa MyolA four-year clinical follow-up and muscle MRI study in FSHD towards trials readiness
TorriF AringhieriG CaldararoL OrtoriS SicilianoG RicciG 1Clinica Neurologica, Univertsità di Pisa, Pisa, Italy162023421 Suppl 14141–113©2023 Gaetano Conte Academy - Mediterranean Society of Myology, Naples, ItalyThis is an open access article distributed in accordance with the CC-BY-NC-ND (Creative Commons Attribution-NonCommercial-NoDerivatives 4.0 International) license.
