## Abstract
A 61-year-woman came to our attention for fatigue, progressive limbs weakness and dropped head. Parents were native from different geographic regions and family history was negative for neuromuscular symptoms. The patient reported two episodes of cyanosis with dyspnea in infancy, recurrent fatigue and a transient period (two months) of deficit of arms elevation after a pregnancy at 26 yo and no other symptoms until 43 yo, when she noted continuous muscle weakness. Since 3 years before she reported nocturnal hypoventilation with the need of NIV. Neurological examination showed weakness of neck extensors (MRC 4), waddling gait with lumbar hyperlordosis, incomplete arms abduction, deficit of finger extension (MRC 3), leg extension (MRC 3) and leg abduction (MRC 4). A mild calf hypertrophy was observed.
Serum CPK was normal. Patients underwent a conventional EMG with cMAP response on all the motor nerves examined. Repetitive stimulation showed a decremental pattern at abductor digiti minimi. An NGS panel for congenital myasthenic syndromes and muscular dystrophy showed COLQ composite heterozygosity (c.367G > C and c.1281C > T) compatible with the diagnosis congenital myasthenic syndrome (CMS). While c.1281C > T mutation has been reported in literature as causative in homozygosity or composite heterozygosity, c.367G > C variant was never reported in healthy controls or in other affected patients. Three different predictive analysis software reported the mutation as damaging. Patients started therapy with albuterol at 2 mg TID with rapid improvement of fatigue and quality of life.

Collection date 2023 Jun.

## Abstract
The aim of this study was to assess the frequency, the clinical characteristics and the impact on the quality of life of LS in patients with MG.
Patients with a diagnosis of MG with the involvement of extraocular muscle and Acetylcholine Receptor Antibodies (AChR-Ab) consecutively admitted at Policlinico A. Gemelli (Rome), from December 2021 to January 2023 were enrolled. Patients with ophtalmological disorders, migraine and other neurological diseases were excluded
Sixty-seven patients with MG were recruited (males = 47, 70%). Sixty age- and sex-matched healthy subjects (HS) served as control (males = 35, 58%).
Patients and HS underwent the Visual light sensitivity Questionnaire-8 (VLSQ-8) and pupillometric examination. QMG, MG-ADL and MG-QoL-15 scales were administered to MG patients.
The frequency of LS was significantly higher in patients (28/67, 42%), compared with HS (0/60, 0%). In MG patients that the severity of LS, measured by VLSQ8, predicted a higher MG-QoL15, MG-ADL and QMG scale score. LS was not associated with pyridostigmine therapy or with a specific subtype of MG.
Pupillometry revealed a significant reduction of maximun constriction velocity (MCV) and dilatation velocity (DV) in MG patients compared with HS.
Our data suggest that LS represents a characteristic and disabling symptom of MG with an impact on the quality of life of patients, and that it correlates with disease severity.

Collection date 2023 Jun.

## Abstract
We report our real life experience with the neonatal Fc receptor (FcRn) inhibitor Efgartigimod (EFG) in 19 pts affected with generalized MG. EFG was administered according to the GENERATIVE protocol (consisting of a Fixed period of 2 treament cycles - 4 infusions at weekly intervals-, followed by a Flexible period during which EFG was given in case of clinical worsening). 8 pts were AChR-Ab+, 4 MuSK+, 2 LRP4+, and 5 were seronegative. Clinical outcomes were evaluated by means of the MG-ADL, QMG, and MGC rating scales. 53% of patients needed 3 treament cycles, 26% 4 and 21% were given 5 cycles along a clinical follow-up of 16 months (Nov 2021-Feb 2022). Meaningful improvement was observed at the end of each treatment cycle with all the clinical scores adopted. The impact of EFG was even more evident considering the course of the disease before enrollment in the treatment protocol. During the year preceding EFG, 8/19 (42%) patients were hospitalized, and 15/19 (79%) needed variable treatment cycles with Plex or IVIG; 3/19 (16%) were admitted to the ICU for respiratory insufficiency. During EFG, none of the patients was hospitalized and only 1 required immunomodulation with Plex and IVIG. No major side effects or infusion related reactions occurred. Our results confirm that EFG is not only effective, but also able to modify considerably the course of the disease, strenghtening the role for FcRn inhibition in the management of generalized MG, and set the basis for optimization of long-term protocols.

Collection date 2023 Jun.
